1	# Incident Copilot: Technical Design Document
2	
3	| | |
4	|---|---|
5	| **Requirements** | [`PRD.md`](PRD.md) (Draft v1). This doc covers *how*; the PRD covers *what* and *why*. |
6	| **Status** | Final for build |
7	| **Date** | 2026-10-15 |

10	
11	## 1. Decisions log
12	
13	These are binding for the build. "Dev" marks a deliberate deviation from the PRD.
14	
15	| # | Decision | Rationale |
16	|---|---|---|
17	| D1 | Full PRD scope, built in strict Must→Should order with a cut line (§17). Anything not built is documented in the README as designed but not built. | 2-day window |
18	| D2 | Rate limits, quotas and the budget guard ship on **Day 2** (M3), as the PRD orders them. *Accepted risk:* the `workers.dev` URL is unprotected for about a day. Mitigation: don't share the URL until WP-17 lands. | The user chose to keep PRD ordering |
19	| D3 | `DiagnosisWorkflow` extends the Agents SDK's **`AgentWorkflow`** (`agents@0.28.1`, pinned exactly). It uses `runWorkflow`, `waitForApproval` and `approveWorkflow`/`rejectWorkflow`. `WatchWorkflow` and `NotifyWorkflow` are plain `WorkflowEntrypoint`s because cron or the Agent starts them without needing a callback. | Removes glue code. Logic stays in plain modules (PRD R2). |
20	| D4 | **Streaming:** the final Workflow step `step.do("narrate")` calls the Agent over RPC (`narrate(runId)`). The Agent calls the LLM with `stream: true`, sends deltas over the WebSocket, and stores the final text. It's idempotent per `runId` and counts as LLM turn 4. | Real streaming that stays durable (FR-3) |
21	| D5 | The **structured diagnosis comes from a `submit_findings` tool call** that ends planning: `{category, confidence, evidence[], actions[]}`, validated with zod. Narration only writes prose from those fixed findings. If the call is missing or invalid, the rule-based classifier fills the fields. | The structure is validated before any text streams, and the eval is deterministic |
22	| D6 | **Tools are pinned to the incident's origin.** `http_probe` accepts any path on the incident host. `dns_lookup` and `cert_info` accept the host or CNAME targets found in this incident's DNS evidence. `provider_status` uses a fixed list. A different host needs a new incident, and the UI offers a button. | Prevents use as an open proxy or a prompt-injection pivot |
23	| D7 | The demo is served at **`/w/<workspaceId>/`** as the PRD specifies. *Accepted risk:* the workspace ID is the session credential and appears in a public URL, prompts and logs. It's documented in the README security section. | The user chose to keep the PRD design |
24	| D8 | **Incident links are view-only for non-owners.** Connections are tagged `owner` or `viewer` from the cookie in `onConnect`. Every mutating callable requires `owner`. | Stops quota theft and approvals from someone who only has a link |
25	| D9 | **A deterministic target parser** reads the first message. It takes a URL first, then a hostname token, then the keyword "demo" (which maps to the demo URL). Otherwise it returns a 400 asking for a host. No LLM is involved, and the UI has a "Use demo service" chip. | Predictable, free, and keeps the first check card under 2 s |
26	| D10 | **Asset sub-probe:** when `http_probe` gets an HTML response, it also fetches the first same-origin script or stylesheet once and records the asset's cache headers in the same evidence. | Makes `cache_misconfiguration` detectable by the rules and the eval |
27	| D11 | Dev: **post-diagnosis monitoring runs on Agent scheduler ticks** (`this.schedule`), not inside Workflows. That covers the 15 s ticks after an action, the 60 s ticks for `recheck_burst`, and watch cron results feeding the same counter. Workflows end after verify (Diagnosis) or notify (Watch). | The 50-subrequest limit per Workflow run on Free |
28	| D12 | **Cron checks due watches inline**, with `LIMIT 6` per tick and a subrequest counter. `WatchWorkflow` starts only on failure. Dev: a **global cap of 30 active watches** across the app. | Fits the 50 subrequests per invocation (6 watches × up to 7 subrequests each) |
29	| D13 | **One planning run and one pending approval per incident.** The input is disabled while a run is active, and the server returns 409 for a second send. Follow-ups are allowed while an approval is pending. A different new proposal **supersedes** the pending one (`rejectWorkflow(old, "superseded")`). The same proposal is deduplicated. Approve and Reject are idempotent. | No conflicting actions and no piling up of cards |
30	| D14 | **A single "LLM run" quota.** New incidents, follow-ups and postmortems all count against 3/min (Rate Limiting binding) and 10/hour (D1). Voice has its own limit. Hitting a limit shows the time until reset. | Directly protects neurons |
31	| D15 | Dev: **Terraform uses local state.** CI only runs `fmt -check` and `validate`, and `apply` is run by hand from a laptop. CI does run D1 migrations and `wrangler deploy` on `main`. | Infrastructure is created once. No extra account needed. |
32	| D16 | **The sidebar polls `GET /api/incidents` every 10 s** while the tab is visible. New unread rows trigger a badge and a browser notification. | No extra DO, and the D1 index already exists |
33	| D17 | **UI uses Kumo** (`@cloudflare/kumo`, Cloudflare's design system as used on cloudflare.com). Fonts are open-licensed look-alikes, with Cloudflare's commercial fonts named first in the stack (§13.1). | The site's fonts are commercially licensed |
34	| D18 | Dev: **webhooks fire only for watch-originated incidents** (opened, mitigated, resolved). On-demand incidents have the user present, so the in-app channel covers them. Sending happens in `NotifyWorkflow`, whose instance ID `notify-<incidentId>-<state>` makes it at most once per state change. | FR-54 dedupe comes from the Workflow ID being unique |

36	## 2. Stack and versions
37	
38	| Concern | Choice |
39	|---|---|
40	| Package manager and layout | pnpm with a single package. The main Worker and React client are built by **Vite with `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` 1.64**. `demo-service/` is a second Worker, included through `auxiliaryWorkers` so the Service Binding works in `vite dev`. |
41	| Runtime | `wrangler` 4.151, `compatibility_date: "2026-10-01"`, flags `nodejs_compat` and `global_fetch_strictly_public` (lets probes reach our own `workers.dev` demo without error 1042, and keeps fetch on the public internet) |
42	| Server | `agents` 0.28.1 (pinned), `hono` 4.13 for `/api/*`, `zod` 4.6 |
43	| Client | React 19, `wouter` 3 (routes `/` and `/i/:id`), `agents/react` `useAgent`, `@cloudflare/kumo` 2.14 (exact pin), Tailwind v4, `@phosphor-icons/react`, `@fontsource-variable/hanken-grotesk` and `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono` |
44	| Tests | `vitest` with `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` 0.23 |
45	| LLM | Workers AI `@cf/openai/gpt-oss-120b` (primary) and `@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash` (fallback) through AI Gateway `incident-copilot`. Voice uses `@cf/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo`. |
46	
47	## 3. Architecture
48	
49	```
50	Browser (React + Kumo) ──HTTPS /api/*──▶ Main Worker (Hono) ──▶ D1
51	        │                                  │  ├─ getAgentByName(IncidentAgent, id).init()
52	        └──WS /agents/incident-agent/<id>──▶ IncidentAgent DO (SQLite)  ◀─ RPC ─┐
53	                                              │ runWorkflow / approveWorkflow   │
54	                                              ▼                                 │
55	                                       DiagnosisWorkflow (AgentWorkflow) ───────┘
56	                                              │ env.AI (via AI Gateway) · tools → internet
57	                                              │ env.DEMO (Service Binding → DemoAdmin RPC)
58	Cron (every 1 min) ─▶ scheduled(): due watches → quick check → WatchWorkflow (on fail)
59	WatchWorkflow ─▶ IncidentAgent.init(watch) · NotifyWorkflow ─▶ Slack/Discord
60	Demo Worker: public /w/<ws>/… (reads DemoState DO) · DemoAdmin WorkerEntrypoint (binding only)
61	```
62	
63	| Component | Owns | Doesn't own |
64	|---|---|---|
65	| Main Worker `worker/index.ts` | Routing order: `routeAgentRequest`, then Hono `/api/*`, then assets. Workspace cookie, target parsing, quotas, voice, `scheduled()`. | Incident state |
66	| `IncidentAgent` | The source of truth for one incident: SQL tables, synced `IncidentState`, connection roles, transitions, narration, approvals, monitor ticks, postmortem, D1 index sync | Long tool loops (those belong to the Workflow) |
67	| `DiagnosisWorkflow` | One bounded run: planning turns, tool steps, findings, narration step, approval wait, action, verify | Monitoring after verify (D11) |
68	| `WatchWorkflow` | Confirm 2 of 3 failures, then open an incident, start diagnosis and trigger notify | Quick checks (cron does those) |
69	| `NotifyWorkflow` | One retried step that sends a webhook (D18) | |
70	| Demo Worker | Public demo pages per workspace and mode, `DemoState` DO, `DemoAdmin` RPC | |
71	
72	**Routing:** `run_worker_first: ["/api/*", "/agents/*"]`, `not_found_handling: "single-page-application"`.
73

138	- **Secrets:** `WEBHOOK_KEY` (base64, 32 bytes), set with `wrangler secret put`.
139	- **Local:** `.dev.vars` sets `LLM_MODE=mock`.
140	- **Demo wrangler:** `name: incident-copilot-demo`, a `DemoState` DO with a `new_sqlite_classes` migration, and `WorkerEntrypoint` `DemoAdmin` exported alongside the default fetch handler.

500	
501	## 12. Frontend
502	
503	### 12.1 Layout (`AppShell`)
504	
505	- **Header:** wordmark "Incident Copilot", `BudgetBanner`, and a GitHub link. Don't use the Cloudflare logo; "Built on Cloudflare" in the footer is enough.
506	- **Left `Sidebar`:** "New incident" and the incident list (status badge, unread dot, polled every 10 s), followed by `WatchlistPanel` and `WebhookSettings`.
507	- **Main area:** `ChatPane`.
508	- **Right column:** `DemoPanel`, which collapses below the `lg` breakpoint.
509	
510	### 12.2 Components
511	
512	- **`ChatPane`:** messages, check cards inline in run order, approval cards, and a composer that's disabled while `activeRunId` is set. The empty state shows chips for "Use demo service" and "diagnose cloudflare.com". The composer includes `VoiceButton`.
513	- **`CheckCard`:** tool icon, label, and a `StatusBadge` with icon **and** text (`Pending`, `Running` with a `Loader`, `✓ Pass`, `✕ Fail`, `Unavailable`). It shows a summary and expands with `Collapsible` to show evidence in `Code`, which is fetched lazily with `getEvidence`. Clicking an `[http-1]` chip scrolls to the card and flashes an orange ring.
514	- **`ApprovalCard`:** the action title, why it's proposed (cause plus refs), and **Approve** (orange primary) and **Reject** buttons. It shows the resolved state: approved, rejected, expired or superseded. For viewers, the buttons are hidden and `ReadOnlyBanner` is shown.
515	- **Status chip in the incident header:** `investigating`, `awaiting approval`, `mitigated` or `resolved`, always with text. It's followed by Resolve and Postmortem buttons.
516	
517	### 12.3 State
518	
519	`useIncident(id)` wraps `useAgent<IncidentState>`. It merges `delta` broadcasts into a streaming buffer keyed by `messageId` and exposes the callables. `useIncidents()` polls the list and fires `useNotifications()` when a new unread row appears (Notification API, only after permission is granted from a button click).
520	
521	### 12.4 Design system (D17)
522	
523	`web/styles.css`:
524	
525	```css
526	@source "../node_modules/@cloudflare/kumo/dist/**/*.{js,jsx,ts,tsx}";
527	@import "@cloudflare/kumo/styles/tailwind";   /* must precede tailwindcss */
528	@import "tailwindcss";
529	@import "@fontsource-variable/hanken-grotesk";
530	@import "@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono";
531	
532	@theme {
533	  --font-sans:    "STK Bureau Sans", "Hanken Grotesk Variable", system-ui, sans-serif;
534	  --font-display: "FT Kunst Grotesk", "Hanken Grotesk Variable", system-ui, sans-serif;
535	  --font-mono:    "Apercu Mono Pro", "JetBrains Mono Variable", ui-monospace, monospace;
536	  --color-cf-orange: #ff5e1f;  --color-cf-orange-hover: #ff7038;  --color-cf-orange-muted: #bf4c14;
537	  --radius-xs: .125rem; --radius-sm: .25rem; --radius-md: .375rem; --radius-lg: .5rem; --radius-xl: .75rem;
538	}
539	:root {
540	  --cf-bg-100: light-dark(#ffffff, #151414);  --cf-bg-200: light-dark(#fdfdfc, #191817);
541	  --cf-bg-300: light-dark(#f9f7f6, #2a2927);  --cf-fg: light-dark(#262626, #f0e3de);
542	  --cf-fg-muted: light-dark(#737373, #a3a3a3); --cf-border: light-dark(#f0e3de, #f0e3de20);
543	}
544	#root { isolation: isolate; }
545	```
546	
547	- Token values come from cloudflare.com's live CSS (2026-10-14).
548	- **Never use Tailwind `dark:` variants.** Kumo uses `light-dark()`, and `theme.ts` sets `data-mode` on `<html>` from `prefers-color-scheme` and listens for changes.
549	- Headings use `font-display` at weight 500. Hostnames, evidence IDs and raw evidence use `font-mono`.
550	- Orange is only for primary CTAs (Send, Approve) and highlight rings. Everything else uses Kumo's neutral variants.
551	- **Kumo components used:** `Sidebar`, `Button`, `Badge`, `LayerCard`, `Surface`, `Collapsible`, `Code`, `Loader`, `Banner`, `Toast`, `Dialog`, `ClipboardText`, `Input`, `SensitiveInput`, `Select`/`Radio`, `Table`, `Tooltip`, `Empty`. Before building, run `npx @cloudflare/kumo ls` and read `node_modules/@cloudflare/kumo/ai/USAGE.md` for exact props.
552	- **Accessibility:** every control works from the keyboard, the composer submits with Enter (Shift+Enter for a new line), Approve and Reject are real `<button>`s, the stream region has `aria-live="polite"`, and status is never shown by color alone.
553	
554	## 13. Observability
555	
556	- `log.ts` emits structured JSON `{event, incident_id, workflow_id, step, ms, ...}` to Workers Logs (`observability.enabled`).
557	- AI Gateway logs every LLM call. The incident timeline is the audit trail the user sees.
558	- In WP-13, check CPU time per step and request in Workers Logs (R9).
559	
560	## 14. Testing
561	
562	| Layer | Files | Covers |
563	|---|---|---|
564	| Unit (node) | `ssrf.test.ts` | A table of 40+ IPv4/IPv6 cases (blocked and allowed), schemes, ports, IP literals, hop re-checks |
565	| | `target.test.ts` | URL, then hostname, then "demo", then need_target; "dot" normalization |
566	| | `dns.test.ts`, `http.test.ts` | DoH JSON parsing and the mismatch flag; samples, hops, loop detection, header picks, asset extraction, read cap |
567	| | `classify.test.ts` | One fixture per category plus precedence between rules |
568	| | `citations.test.ts` | Unknown IDs and uncited factual sentences are removed |
569	| | `transitions.test.ts` | Every legal edge, and illegal edges throw |
570	| | `webhook.test.ts` | Allowlist accept/reject, AES-GCM round trip, masking |
571	| | `origin.test.ts` | Origin-pin scope rules |
572	| Integration (`vitest-pool-workers`, mocked outbound fetch, `LLM_MODE=mock`) | `agent.test.ts` | `init`, owner/viewer roles, idempotent `recordCheck`, index sync |
573	| | `diagnosis.test.ts` | Scripted mock LLM: tools, then findings, then narrate. Invalid call ×2 → rule path. LLM unavailable → rule path. Budget cap. |
574	| | `approval.test.ts` | Propose → approve → `setMode` called → verify → `mitigated` → 2 ticks → `resolved`. Reject → `investigating`. Supersede. |
575	| | `cron.test.ts` | Due-watch query with LIMIT and caps; failure starts WatchWorkflow; duplicate ID ignored |
576	| | `demo.test.ts` | Every mode's status, headers and timing, through `SELF` and the `DemoAdmin` RPC |
577	
578	Workflow tests use the pool's Workflow introspection APIs. Retrying events with `waitForEvent` and the timeout path are verified in WP-02.
579	
580	## 15. CI/CD and IaC
581	
582	- **`ci.yml`** (on PRs): `pnpm i --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test`, then `terraform fmt -check` and `terraform init -backend=false && terraform validate` (D15).
583	- **`deploy.yml`** (on push to `main`): runs the tests, `wrangler d1 migrations apply DB --remote` and `vite build`, deploys `demo-service`, then deploys main, then runs a smoke test.
584	  - Deploys use `cloudflare/wrangler-action`.
585	  - The smoke test calls `GET /api/health`, then `GET /api/session`, then `POST /api/demo/mode healthy`, then `POST /api/incidents "diagnose demo"`. It polls the snapshot until `activeRunId` is null and expects `category = healthy`. Run it with `LLM_MODE` live but only once per deploy, or skip the LLM with a `?smoke=rule` flag that forces `rule_based`.
586	- **Secrets:** `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` live in GitHub. `WEBHOOK_KEY` is set once with `wrangler secret put`.
587	- **Terraform (`infra/`, local state, gitignored):** Cloudflare provider v5 with `cloudflare_d1_database`, plus the AI Gateway resource if the provider supports it. Otherwise the gateway is created in the dashboard and the README says so (R1). The `database_id` output is copied into `wrangler.jsonc` once. Wrangler owns everything else (§5.1).
588	
589	## 16. LLM eval (`eval/run.ts`)
590	
591	`pnpm eval --base <url>` works like this:
592	1. Get a session cookie.
593	2. For each case (`slow` → `high_latency`, `errors` → `origin_5xx`, `redirect_loop` → `redirect_loop`, `bad_cache` → `cache_misconfiguration`, `healthy` → `healthy`): set the mode, create an incident with "what's wrong with my demo service?", and poll the snapshot until it's done.
594	3. Score each case on three checks: ① the category is correct, ② at least one citation points to a decisive check that exists (`http-*`), ③ `restore_demo_service` is proposed (for healthy, it must **not** be proposed).
595	4. Write `eval/results.md` with a table and the model used. The target is at least 4 of 5 passing.
596	
597	A full run costs about 3k neurons, so don't run it on a review day.
598	
599	## 17. Build plan (2 days)
600	
601	The estimates are focused hours with heavy AI help. **Day 1 is heavy (about 14 h).** If it runs over, WP-12 and WP-13 move to the start of Day 2 ahead of all Should items. Commit after each WP, and export the session transcript into `prompts/` when the day ends.
602

608	| 02 | **Spike:** gpt-oss-120b and glm request/response and tool-call shapes, streaming through the AI Gateway binding, timeout behavior of `waitForApproval`, whether DO RPC and D1 count as subrequests. Record fixtures for `mock.ts`. | 0.75 | 01 | Notes added to TDD §7.4 and §5.2, plus fixtures in `test/fixtures/` |

## Approved Plan:
# WP-01 Scaffold + PROMPTS.md log

## Context
Day 1 of the 2-day Incident Copilot build (TASKS.md). The repo has only PRD.md, TDD.md and TASKS.md, and git isn't initialised yet. WP-01 sets up the project skeleton every later WP builds on. The user also wants every prompt in this chat logged. That goes into the PRD's existing `PROMPTS.md` deliverable (no separate PROMPT_HISTORY.md), which WP-16 tidies up later.

## Decisions from the grilling
1. **Prompt log = `PROMPTS.md`** at the repo root, appended every turn. Each entry has the time, the WP tag, the user's exact message, and one line per question the user answered. WP-16 later adds the curated overview and the links to `prompts/` transcripts.
2. **Versions:** follow TDD §2 exactly, except **vitest 4.1.x** (vitest-pool-workers 0.23 needs ^4.1) and **TypeScript 6.0.x** (typescript-eslint needs <6.1). Add **echarts ^6** for Kumo's peer.
3. **Git:** `git init` on `main`, then create a **private** GitHub repo `cf_ai_incident_copilot` with `gh` now, and commit and push after each WP.
4. **Config grows per WP:** WP-01's `wrangler.jsonc` only includes what exists. D1 is created later with `wrangler d1 create` (WP-03), and Terraform imports it if WP-25 gets built (deviation from D15).

## Steps
1. **TASKS.md:** set WP-01 to 🔄, Started = now, and update the "Now" section. Add rows to the decisions log for decisions 2–4.
2. **PROMPTS.md:** create it with a short header and an entry for this first prompt plus the 5 answers.
3. **git + GitHub:** `git init -b main`. Add a `.gitignore` covering node_modules, dist, .wrangler, `.dev.vars`, `infra/*.tfstate*` and `.terraform`. Run `gh repo create cf_ai_incident_copilot --private --source . --remote origin`.
4. **package.json** (pnpm, single package): `react`, `react-dom`, `wouter`, `@cloudflare/kumo@2.14.0` (exact), `@phosphor-icons/react`, `echarts`, `@fontsource-variable/hanken-grotesk`, `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono`, `hono`, `zod`, and `agents@0.28.1` (exact). Dev dependencies: `vite`, `@vitejs/plugin-react`, `@cloudflare/vite-plugin@1.64`, `wrangler@4.151`, `tailwindcss@4`, `@tailwindcss/vite`, `typescript@~6.0`, `vitest@~4.1`, `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers@0.23`, `eslint`, `typescript-eslint`, `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`, `@cloudflare/workers-types` (or `wrangler types`). Scripts: `dev`, `build`, `typecheck`, `lint`, `test`, `deploy`.
5. **Configs:** `vite.config.ts` (react, tailwind, `cloudflare({ auxiliaryWorkers: [{ configPath: "demo-service/wrangler.jsonc" }] })`), `tsconfig.json` plus project refs for web, worker and node, `eslint.config.js`, and `vitest.config.ts` (pool-workers).
6. **`wrangler.jsonc` (main):** name, `main: worker/index.ts`, compat date `2026-10-01`, flags `nodejs_compat` and `global_fetch_strictly_public`, `assets` with SPA fallback and `run_worker_first`, observability, vars, and the `DEMO` service binding → `DemoAdmin`. No AI, D1, DO, Workflow or rate-limit bindings yet.
7. **`demo-service/wrangler.jsonc` + `demo-service/src/index.ts`:** name `incident-copilot-demo`, a default fetch handler stub, and an exported stub `DemoAdmin extends WorkerEntrypoint` (the DemoState DO comes in WP-11).
8. **`worker/index.ts`:** a minimal Hono app with `GET /api/health` → `{ok:true}`, and everything else falls through to assets. Add `worker/env.ts`.
9. **Web:** `index.html`, `web/main.tsx`, `App.tsx`, `styles.css` (copied verbatim from TDD §12.4), and `theme.ts` (sets `data-mode` on `<html>` from `prefers-color-scheme` and listens for changes). First run `npx @cloudflare/kumo ls` and read `node_modules/@cloudflare/kumo/ai/USAGE.md` for the exact props.
10. **`web/components/AppShell.tsx`:** a header with the "Incident Copilot" wordmark (`font-display` 500) and a GitHub link to the repo, a Kumo `Sidebar` on the left (with a placeholder "New incident"), the main area, a right column hidden below `lg`, and a "Built on Cloudflare" footer (no logo). No `dark:` variants.
11. **Tests:** `test/unit/theme.test.ts` (data-mode switching) and `test/integration/health.test.ts` (`SELF.fetch('/api/health')`), so `pnpm test` has real coverage.
12. **Finish:** commit `WP-01: scaffold` with a Co-Authored-By trailer, push, then mark WP-01 ✅ in TASKS.md with the hash and hours, set Next = WP-02, and add the PROMPTS.md entry. Commit and push that board update.

## Critical files
`package.json`, `vite.config.ts`, `wrangler.jsonc`, `demo-service/wrangler.jsonc`, `worker/index.ts`, `web/styles.css`, `web/theme.ts`, `web/components/AppShell.tsx`, `TASKS.md`, `PROMPTS.md`.

## Verification
- `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` all pass.
- Run `pnpm dev` in the background, then `curl localhost:5173/api/health` → `{ok:true}` and `curl /` returns the SPA HTML. Check that the demo auxiliary worker boots without errors.
- Check light and dark: load the page in a browser (or the `run` skill) with both colour schemes and confirm the tokens switch and the shell layout renders (right column hidden below `lg`).
- `gh repo view` shows a private repo with the pushed commit.

## Memory
Save a feedback memory: the user prefers questions and explanations in plain, simple words.

Action:

# @cloudflare/kumo — AI Usage Guide

> Cloudflare's React component library built on [Base UI](https://base-ui.com) + Tailwind CSS v4.

## Quick Start

### CSS setup (required)

In your main CSS file (e.g. `global.css`), add **all three lines in this order**:

```css
@source "path/to/node_modules/@cloudflare/kumo/dist/**/*.{js,jsx,ts,tsx}";
@import "@cloudflare/kumo/styles";
@import "tailwindcss";
```

- **`@source`** — Tells Tailwind to scan kumo's compiled JS for class names. Without this, kumo component internals will be unstyled. Adjust the relative path so it resolves to your project's `node_modules/`.
- **Import order matters** — `@cloudflare/kumo/styles` must come **before** `@import "tailwindcss"` so kumo's `@theme` tokens are registered first.

### Component usage

```tsx
import { Button, Input, Dialog } from "@cloudflare/kumo";
```

## Critical Rules

1. **Semantic tokens only** — use `bg-kumo-base`, `text-kumo-default`, etc. Never use raw Tailwind colors (`bg-blue-500`).
2. **No `dark:` variant** — light/dark mode is handled automatically via CSS `light-dark()`. Never add `dark:` prefixes.
3. **Merge classes with `cn()`** — import from `@cloudflare/kumo` and use it to combine class names.
4. **Compound components** — many components use a dot-notation API: `<Dialog.Root>`, `<Dialog.Trigger>`, etc.

## Component Quick Reference

| Component          | Category   | Description                           | Key Props                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------ | ---------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `Badge`            | Display    | Status/label indicator                | `variant`: primary, secondary, destructive, outline, beta                                                                      |
| `Banner`           | Feedback   | Page-level alert message              | `variant`: default, alert, error; `icon`, `onDismiss`                                                                          |
| `Breadcrumbs`      | Display    | Navigation breadcrumb trail           | Compound: `.Link` (with `href`), `.Separator`, `.Current`; `size`: sm, base                                                    |
| `Button`           | Action     | Clickable action trigger              | `variant`: primary, secondary, ghost, destructive, secondary-destructive, outline; `size`, `shape`, `icon`, `loading`          |
| `Checkbox`         | Input      | Toggle or multi-select control        | `checked`, `onCheckedChange`, `label`, `indeterminate`                                                                         |
| `Checkbox.Group`   | Input      | Group of checkboxes with fieldset     | `legend`, `orientation`, `allValues`, `value`, `onValueChange`                                                                 |
| `ClipboardText`    | Action     | Copy-to-clipboard text display        | `value`, `label`                                                                                                               |
| `Code`             | Display    | Inline code or multi-line code block  | `children`; use `CodeBlock` for multi-line with copy                                                                           |
| `Collapsible`      | Display    | Expandable/collapsible section        | `title`, `defaultOpen`                                                                                                         |
| `Combobox`         | Input      | Autocomplete input with dropdown      | `items`, `value`, `onValueChange`, `multiple`, `label`                                                                         |
| `CommandPalette`   | Navigation | Spotlight-style search overlay        | `.Root`, `.Input`, `.List`, `.Item`, `.ResultItem`                                                                             |
| `DateRangePicker`  | Input      | Dual-calendar date range selector     | `onStartDateChange`, `onEndDateChange`, `size`, `timezone`                                                                     |
| `Dialog`           | Overlay    | Modal dialog with backdrop            | `size`: sm, base, lg, xl; uses `.Root`, `.Trigger`, `.Title`, `.Close`                                                         |
| `DropdownMenu`     | Overlay    | Context/action menu                   | `.Trigger`, `.Content`, `.Item`; item `variant`: default, danger                                                               |
| `Empty`            | Display    | Empty state placeholder               | `icon`, `title`, `description`, `commandLine`, `contents`                                                                      |
| `Field`            | Input      | Form field wrapper with label/error   | `label`, `description`, `error`, `required`, `labelTooltip`                                                                    |
| `Grid`             | Layout     | Responsive CSS grid                   | `variant`: 2up, side-by-side, 2-1, 1-2, 1-3up, 3up, 4up, 1-2-4up; `gap`: none, sm, base, lg                                    |
| `Input`            | Input      | Text input field                      | `size`: xs, sm, base, lg; `variant`: default, error; `label`, `error`                                                          |
| `Label`            | Other      | Form label with optional tooltip      | `children`, `tooltip`, `required`                                                                                              |
| `LayerCard`        | Display    | Card with header and collapsible body | `title`, `tag`, `actions`, `icon`                                                                                              |
| `Link`             | Other      | Styled anchor/router link             | `variant`: inline, current, plain; `href`, `external`                                                                          |
| `Loader`           | Feedback   | Animated loading spinner              | `size`: number (default 16)                                                                                                    |
| `MenuBar`          | Navigation | Horizontal icon toolbar               | `isActive`, `options: { icon, tooltip, onClick }[]`                                                                            |
| `Meter`            | Display    | Progress/usage meter bar              | `value`, `max`, `label`, `size`                                                                                                |
| `Pagination`       | Navigation | Page navigation controls              | `page`, `setPage`, `perPage`, `totalCount`; `controls`: full, simple                                                           |
| `Popover`          | Overlay    | Popup content anchored to trigger     | `.Trigger`, `.Content`, `.Title`, `.Description`, `.Close`; `side`, `align`                                                    |
| `Radio`            | Input      | Single-select radio group             | `.Group` with `legend`, `.Item` with `label`, `value`                                                                          |
| `Select`           | Input      | Dropdown select menu                  | `value`, `onValueChange`, `label`, `hideLabel` (default: true); children: `<Select.Option value="…">`                          |
| `SensitiveInput`   | Input      | Input with show/hide toggle           | `label`, `size`, `variant`                                                                                                     |
| `Surface`          | Layout     | Themed container panel                | `variant`: flat, raised; `as` for polymorphic rendering                                                                        |
| `Switch`           | Input      | Toggle switch control                 | `checked`, `onCheckedChange`, `label`, `size`                                                                                  |
| `Table`            | Display    | Data table with selection             | `.Header`, `.Head`, `.Body`, `.Row`, `.Cell`, `.Footer`, `.CheckCell`, `.CheckHead`, `.ResizeHandle`; `layout`: auto, fixed    |
| `Tabs`             | Navigation | Tabbed navigation                     | `tabs: { value, label, render? }[]`; `variant`: segmented, underline; `value`, `onValueChange`                                 |
| `Text`             | Display    | Themed text with semantic variants    | `variant`: heading1, heading2, heading3, body, secondary, success, error, mono, mono-secondary; `size`: xs, sm, base, lg; `as` |
| `Toast` / `Toasty` | Feedback   | Toast notification system             | Wrap app with `<Toasty>`, use `Toast.useToastManager().notify()`                                                               |
| `Tooltip`          | Overlay    | Hover/focus tooltip                   | `content`, `side`, `align`, `asChild`                                                                                          |

## Semantic Token Reference

### Surface Hierarchy

| Token              | Purpose                                                                      |
| ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `bg-kumo-canvas`   | The outermost page background — sits behind everything                       |
| `bg-kumo-base`     | Default component background                                                 |
| `bg-kumo-elevated` | Slightly elevated surface, e.g. `LayerCard.Secondary`                        |
| `bg-kumo-recessed` | Recessed surface with a subtly darker fill, e.g. segmented `Tabs` background |
| `bg-kumo-tint`     | Subtle tinted background for tables or hover states                          |
| `bg-kumo-contrast` | High-contrast, inverted background                                           |

### Brand

| Token                 | Purpose                           |
| --------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| `bg-kumo-brand`       | Primary brand background          |
| `bg-kumo-brand-hover` | Hover state for brand backgrounds |

### Semantic Status Colors

| Token             | Purpose                         |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------- |
| `bg-kumo-info`    | Info indicator (icon, dot, bar) |
| `bg-kumo-success` | Success indicator               |
| `bg-kumo-warning` | Warning indicator               |
| `bg-kumo-danger`  | Error/destructive indicator     |

Use the solid token on icons, status dots, and progress fills. Banners and badges use the solid fills with a reduced opacity.

### Text Colors

| Token                   | Purpose                                                        |
| ----------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `text-kumo-default`     | Primary body text                                              |
| `text-kumo-strong`      | Secondary text with slightly less contrast than default        |
| `text-kumo-subtle`      | Muted text for descriptions, captions, or secondary labels     |
| `text-kumo-inactive`    | Disabled or inactive text                                      |
| `text-kumo-placeholder` | Placeholder text in inputs                                     |
| `text-kumo-inverse`     | Text intended for use on high-contrast or inverted backgrounds |
| `text-kumo-link`        | Link text                                                      |
| `text-kumo-info`        | Info-colored text                                              |
| `text-kumo-success`     | Success-colored text                                           |
| `text-kumo-warning`     | Warning-colored text                                           |
| `text-kumo-danger`      | Error/destructive text                                         |

### Borders & Rings

| Token           | Purpose                                                                                                 |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `kumo-hairline` | A border/ring color to distinguish between flat surfaces where no shadow is present (i.e. `LayerCard`). |
| `kumo-line`     | A thicker border/ring color that defines the edge of an elevated surface alongside a shadow.            |

## Icons

Kumo uses **[Phosphor Icons](https://phosphoricons.com/)** via `@phosphor-icons/react`:

```tsx
import { PlusIcon, TrashIcon, GearIcon } from "@phosphor-icons/react";

<Button icon={PlusIcon}>Add Worker</Button>
<DropdownMenu.Item icon={TrashIcon} variant="danger">Delete</DropdownMenu.Item>
```

## Controlled State Reference

| Component        | Value Prop | Change Callback   | Default Prop     |
| ---------------- | ---------- | ----------------- | ---------------- |
| `Input`          | `value`    | `onChange`        | `defaultValue`   |
| `Select`         | `value`    | `onValueChange`   | `defaultValue`   |
| `Combobox`       | `value`    | `onValueChange`   | `defaultValue`   |
| `Switch`         | `checked`  | `onCheckedChange` | `defaultChecked` |
| `Checkbox`       | `checked`  | `onCheckedChange` | `defaultChecked` |
| `Checkbox.Group` | `value`    | `onValueChange`   | `defaultValue`   |
| `Radio.Group`    | `value`    | `onValueChange`   | `defaultValue`   |
| `Tabs`           | `value`    | `onValueChange`   | `selectedValue`  |

## Common Patterns

### Field Wrapper (label + description + error)

Most input components accept `label`, `description`, and `error` props that automatically wrap the input in a `<Field>`:

```tsx
<Input label="Email" description="We'll never share your email" error="Required" variant="error" />
<Select label="Region" hideLabel={false} error="Please select a region">
  <Select.Option value="us">US East</Select.Option>
  <Select.Option value="eu">EU West</Select.Option>
</Select>
<Combobox label="Tags" items={tags} multiple />
```

> **Note:** `Select` defaults to `hideLabel={true}` (label is screen-reader only). Pass `hideLabel={false}` for a visible label.

### Compound Components

Many components use a compound/dot-notation API via `Object.assign`:

```tsx
<Dialog.Root>
  <Dialog.Trigger render={(p) => <Button {...p}>Open</Button>} />
  <Dialog size="lg" className="p-8">
    <Dialog.Title>Confirm</Dialog.Title>
    <Dialog.Description>Are you sure?</Dialog.Description>
    <Dialog.Close render={(p) => <Button {...p}>Cancel</Button>} />
  </Dialog>
</Dialog.Root>
```

### Loading States

Buttons support a `loading` prop that shows a spinner and disables interaction:

```tsx
<Button loading>Saving...</Button>
```

### Polymorphic Rendering

Some components accept an `as` prop or Base UI's `render` prop:

```tsx
<Surface as="section" variant="raised">Content</Surface>
<Text variant="heading1" as="h2">Custom heading level</Text>
<Dialog.Trigger render={(p) => <Button {...p}>Open</Button>} />
```

## Full Registry

For complete prop details, variant values, Tailwind classes, and code examples for every component, see:

```
@cloudflare/kumo/ai/component-registry.json
```

This JSON file contains structured metadata for all components, optimized for AI/agent consumption.
````

````
Alternatively, you can use the default style export (`@cloudflare/kumo/styles`) which is equivalent to `styles/tailwind`.

If you are **not** using Tailwind CSS, use the standalone build instead (see below) — no `@source` directive is needed.

#### For Non-Tailwind Users (Standalone)

```js
import "@cloudflare/kumo/styles/standalone";
```

This imports a fully compiled CSS file with all Tailwind utilities and Kumo styles pre-compiled. No Tailwind configuration needed!

**What's included in standalone:**

- All Tailwind utility classes used by Kumo components
- Kumo component styles
- Dark mode support (via `data-mode="dark"` attribute)
- All animations and keyframes
- Responsive utilities

**Note:** The standalone CSS is minified and optimized, but will be larger than the Tailwind version since it includes all utilities.

### Base UI Primitives

Kumo bundles [Base UI](https://base-ui.com) and re-exports all primitives for advanced use cases:

```tsx
// Barrel import - imports all primitives (convenient but larger bundle)
import { Popover, Slider, Accordion } from "@cloudflare/kumo/primitives";

// Granular imports - tree-shakeable, smaller bundles (recommended)
import { Popover } from "@cloudflare/kumo/primitives/popover";
import { Slider } from "@cloudflare/kumo/primitives/slider";
import { Accordion } from "@cloudflare/kumo/primitives/accordion";
```

> **Note:** Prefer styled Kumo components when available. Primitives are for custom components not yet in Kumo or cases requiring fine-grained control.
>
> **Performance tip:** Use granular imports (`@cloudflare/kumo/primitives/{name}`) for better tree-shaking and smaller bundle sizes.
````

### Sidebar

Sidebar — responsive navigation panel with expand/collapse support.

**Type:** component

**Import:** `import { Sidebar } from "@cloudflare/kumo";`

**Category:** Other

**Props:**

- `defaultOpen`: boolean
  Initial open state when uncontrolled.
- `open`: boolean
  Controlled open state.
- `variant`: enum [default: sidebar]
  - `"sidebar"`: Standard sidebar with border separator
  - `"floating"`: Floating sidebar with shadow and rounded corners
  - `"inset"`: Inset sidebar within the content area
- `side`: enum [default: left]
  - `"left"`: Left-aligned sidebar
  - `"right"`: Right-aligned sidebar
- `collapsible`: enum [default: icon]
  - `"icon"`: Collapses to show icons only
  - `"offcanvas"`: Slides off screen when collapsed
  - `"none"`: Cannot be collapsed
- `resizable`: boolean
  Enable drag-to-resize on the sidebar edge.
- `defaultWidth`: number
  Initial width in pixels when resizable.
- `minWidth`: number
  Minimum width in pixels when resizing.
- `maxWidth`: number
  Maximum width in pixels when resizing.
- `contained`: boolean
  When true, the collapsed sidebar uses absolute positioning instead of fixed, keeping it scoped inside a bounded parent. Useful for demos and embedded sidebars.
- `peekable`: boolean
  When true, hovering or focusing the collapsed sidebar temporarily expands it. The `state` will be `"peeking"` during the peek. Moving away collapses it back.
- `animationDuration`: number
  Duration of sidebar expand/collapse animation in milliseconds.
- `mobileBreakpoint`: number
  Viewport width (in px) below which the sidebar renders as a mobile dialog sheet instead of the desktop aside rail.
- `children`: ReactNode
  Content — typically `<Sidebar>` + main content.
- `className`: string
  Additional CSS classes for the wrapper div.

**Colors (kumo tokens used):**

`bg-kumo-brand`, `bg-kumo-hairline`, `bg-kumo-line`, `bg-kumo-recessed`, `border-kumo-line`, `ring-kumo-brand`, `text-kumo-default`, `text-kumo-strong`, `text-kumo-subtle`

**Styling:**


**Sub-Components:**

This is a compound component. Use these sub-components:

#### Sidebar.Provider

Provider sub-component

Props:
- `defaultOpen`: boolean - Initial open state when uncontrolled.
- `open`: boolean - Controlled open state.
- `variant`: SidebarVariant - Sidebar layout variant.
- `side`: SidebarSide - Which side the sidebar is on.
- `collapsible`: "icon" | "offcanvas" | "none"
- `resizable`: boolean - Enable drag-to-resize on the sidebar edge.
- `defaultWidth`: number - Initial width in pixels when resizable.
- `minWidth`: number - Minimum width in pixels when resizing.
- `maxWidth`: number - Maximum width in pixels when resizing.
- `contained`: boolean - When true, the collapsed sidebar uses absolute positioning instead of fixed, keeping it scoped inside a bounded parent. Useful for demos and embedded sidebars.
- `peekable`: boolean - When true, hovering or focusing the collapsed sidebar temporarily expands it. The `state` will be `"peeking"` during the peek. Moving away collapses it back.
- `animationDuration`: number - Duration of sidebar expand/collapse animation in milliseconds.
- `mobileBreakpoint`: number - Viewport width (in px) below which the sidebar renders as a mobile dialog sheet instead of the desktop aside rail.
- `children`: ReactNode (required) - Content — typically `<Sidebar>` + main content.
- `className`: string - Additional CSS classes for the wrapper div.

# `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`

The Workers Vitest integration allows you to write Vitest tests that run inside the Workers runtime.
Refer to the [documentation](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/testing/vitest-integration/) and [examples](https://github.com/cloudflare/workers-sdk/tree/main/fixtures/vitest-plugin-examples/) for more information.

- ✅ Supports both **unit tests** and **integration tests**
- 📚 Provides direct access to Workers runtime APIs and bindings
- 📦 Implements isolated per-test storage
- 🔥 Runs tests fully-locally using [Miniflare](https://miniflare.dev/)
- ⚡️ Leverages Vitest's hot-module reloading for near instant reruns
- ↩️ Provides a declarative interface for mocking outbound requests
- 🧩 Supports projects with multiple Workers
[ '.', './types' ]

  resolvedConfig?: {

### 🤖 Claude · 00:37:04

WP-01 is done and pushed to the private repo [cf_ai_incident_copilot](https://github.com/sahilsaleem2907/cf_ai_incident_copilot) (commits `867d8d6` scaffold, `212c804` board update).

**Checks run:**
- `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` all pass (6 tests). `pnpm build` also succeeds.
- `pnpm dev` runs both Workers. `/api/health` returns `{"ok":true}` and other paths like `/i/abc` load the app.
- I took screenshots in light mode, dark mode and at a narrow width. The colours switch correctly and the right column hides on narrow screens.

**What's in place:**
- The app shell: header with the "Incident Copilot" name and GitHub link, sidebar with a "New incident" button, main area, right column, and "Built on Cloudflare" footer.
- Light/dark mode follows your system setting, with no light flash on page load.
- A stub demo service running next to the main app, ready for WP-11.
- Three test groups: plain unit tests, main-app tests, and demo-service tests.

**Changes from the plan:**
- The test package named in the TDD (`@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers`) has been renamed and won't get updates. I switched to its replacement, `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`. It also works with vitest 5, so the vitest 4.1 pin could be lifted later.
- The main app's tests can't start while its link to the demo service points at nothing. They now run against a small stand-in demo service. The real demo service is tested on its own.

Both changes are in the TASKS.md decisions log, along with your four answers.

**Prompt log:** `PROMPTS.md` has your two messages so far and your answers. I'll add each new prompt as we go, and I've saved that as a standing habit, along with your preference for plain-language questions.

**Next is WP-02 (Spike).** It calls Workers AI, so wrangler needs to be logged in to your Cloudflare account. Please run `pnpm exec wrangler login` yourself in a terminal, since it opens a browser.

I logged WP-01 as 0.25 h against the 1.0 h estimate.

````
### `AgentWorkflow<AgentType, Params, ProgressType, Env>`

Base class for Workflows that integrate with Agents.

**Type Parameters:**

- `AgentType` - The Agent class type (for typed RPC)
- `Params` - User params passed to the workflow (optional)
- `ProgressType` - Type for progress reporting (defaults to `DefaultProgress`)
- `Env` - Environment type (defaults to `Cloudflare.Env`)

**Properties:**

- `agent` - Typed stub for calling Agent methods via RPC. For workflows started from sub-agents, this is an RPC-only stub back to the originating facet; use sub-agent routing for HTTP or WebSocket `fetch()` traffic.
- `instanceId` - The workflow instance ID
- `workflowName` - The workflow binding name
- `env` - Environment bindings

**Methods on `this` (non-durable, may repeat on retry):**

| Method                         | Description                                   |
| ------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------- |
| `reportProgress(progress)`     | Report typed progress object to the Agent     |
| `broadcastToClients(message)`  | Broadcast message to all WebSocket clients    |
| `waitForApproval(step, opts?)` | Wait for approval event (throws on rejection) |

**Methods on `step` (durable, idempotent, won't repeat on retry):**

| Method                          | Description                                    |
| ------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| `step.reportComplete(result?)`  | Report successful completion                   |
| `step.reportError(error)`       | Report an error                                |
| `step.sendEvent(event)`         | Send a custom event to the Agent               |
| `step.updateAgentState(state)`  | Replace Agent state (broadcasts to clients)    |
| `step.mergeAgentState(partial)` | Merge into Agent state (broadcasts to clients) |
| `step.resetAgentState()`        | Reset Agent state to initialState              |

**DefaultProgress Type:**

```typescript
type DefaultProgress = {
  step?: string;
  status?: "pending" | "running" | "complete" | "error";
  message?: string;
  percent?: number;
  [key: string]: unknown; // extensible
};
```

### Agent Workflow Methods

Methods added to the `Agent` class:

#### `runWorkflow(workflowName, params, options?)`

Start a workflow and track it in the originating Agent's database.
    // Broadcast progress to all connected clients
    this.broadcast(
      JSON.stringify({
        type: "processing-progress",
        workflowName,
        instanceId,
        progress
      })
    );
  }
}
```

### Human-in-the-Loop Approval

```typescript
// Workflow using the built-in waitForApproval helper
export class ApprovalWorkflow extends AgentWorkflow<MyAgent, RequestParams> {
  async run(event: AgentWorkflowEvent<RequestParams>, step: AgentWorkflowStep) {
    const params = event.payload;

    // Prepare request
    const request = await step.do("prepare", async () => {
      return { ...params, preparedAt: Date.now() };
    });

    // Wait for approval (throws WorkflowRejectedError if rejected)
    await this.reportProgress({
      step: "approval",
      status: "pending",
      percent: 0.5,
      message: "Awaiting approval"
    });
    const approvalData = await this.waitForApproval<{ approvedBy: string }>(
      step,
      { timeout: "7 days" }
    );

    console.log("Approved by:", approvalData?.approvedBy);

    // Execute approved action
    const result = await step.do("execute", async () => {
      return executeRequest(request);
    });

    await step.reportComplete(result);
    return result;
  }
}

// Agent using the built-in approval methods
class MyAgent extends Agent {
  // Approve a waiting workflow
  async handleApproval(instanceId: string, userId: string) {
    await this.approveWorkflow(instanceId, {
      reason: "Approved by admin",
      metadata: { approvedBy: userId }
    });
  }

  // Reject a waiting workflow
  async handleRejection(instanceId: string, reason: string) {
    await this.rejectWorkflow(instanceId, { reason });
  }
}
```

### Durable Task Queue with Retries

```typescript
// Workflow with built-in retry logic
export class ResilientTaskWorkflow extends AgentWorkflow<MyAgent, TaskParams> {
  async run(event: AgentWorkflowEvent<TaskParams>, step: AgentWorkflowStep) {
    const params = event.payload;

    const result = await step.do(
      "call-external-api",
      {
        retries: {
          limit: 5,
          delay: "10 seconds",
          backoff: "exponential"
        },
        timeout: "5 minutes"
      },
      async () => {
        const response = await fetch("https://api.example.com/process", {
          method: "POST",
          body: JSON.stringify(params)
        });

        if (!response.ok) {
          throw new Error(`API error: ${response.status}`);
        }

        return response.json();
      }
    );

    await step.reportComplete(result);
    return result;

# AI Gateway Auto-Creation and Creation Methods

**Auto-creation on first request:**

Yes, AI Gateway auto-creates a default gateway. The documentation states: "If the gateway does not exist, AI Gateway creates it on the first authenticated request." Additionally, "For third-party models, you do not need to specify a gateway — AI Gateway uses `default` as the gateway ID and automatically creates it on the first authenticated request."

**Creation methods:**

The documentation describes three approaches:

1. **Dashboard:** Navigate to AI > AI Gateway, select Create Gateway, enter your gateway name, choose Workers AI billing preference, then select Create.

2. **API:** "Using that API token and Account ID, send a `POST` request to the Cloudflare API."

3. **Automatic (mentioned but not wrangler):** The document does not mention wrangler as a gateway creation tool—only for retrieving authentication credentials.

# Subrequests on Workers Free Plan

Based on the documentation provided, here's what counts toward the 50 subrequest limit on the Free plan:

**Definition**: "A subrequest is any request a Worker makes using the Fetch API or to Cloudflare services like R2, KV, or D1."

This explicitly includes calls to Cloudflare internal services. The documentation states there is a separate quota for "Subrequests to internal services" (limited to 1,000 on Free), but these still count against your primary subrequest limit.

**However, the document does not explicitly mention** whether Durable Objects, D1, Service Bindings, or Workers AI bindings have separate handling beyond what's indicated for "Cloudflare services."

The key quoted passage: "Each subrequest in a redirect chain counts against this limit."

The most direct information available is that the 50-subrequest limit applies to "Fetch API or to Cloudflare services like R2, KV, or D1" — suggesting these internal service calls do count toward your limit, not separately.
```

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

```
# Subrequests Limits

## Account Plan Limits Table

| Limit | Workers Free | Workers Paid |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Subrequests per invocation | 50 | 10,000 (up to 10M) |
| Subrequests to internal services | 1,000 | Matches configured limit (default 10,000) |

## Subrequests Section

A subrequest encompasses any request a Worker initiates through the "Fetch API or to Cloudflare services like R2, KV, or D1."

Each subrequest in redirect chains counts against the limit, meaning "the total number of subrequests may exceed the number of fetch() calls in your code." You can adjust this per-Worker using the `limits` configuration in Wrangler.

There's no fixed time constraint on individual subrequests—as long as the client stays connected, Workers can continue making them. However, upon client disconnect or response completion, pending work may be canceled unless passed to `ctx.waitUntil()`, which "can extend execution for up to 30 seconds."

## Service Bindings and Durable Objects

For Worker-to-Worker communication, the documentation recommends using "Service Bindings to send requests from one Worker to another on your account without going over the Internet." Standard `fetch()` calls between Workers on the same zone without service bindings will fail, though Workers accept requests sent to Custom Domains.
```

# GLM-4.7-Flash Input Format & Function Calling

## Input Format

The model accepts inputs via `env.AI.run()` with these parameters:

- **messages**: Array of message objects with `role` and `content` fields
- **stream**: Boolean to enable server-sent events (optional)
- **tools**: Array of tool definitions for function calling (supported)

# Routing env.AI.run Through AI Gateway

To route `env.AI.run()` through AI Gateway, pass a `gateway` object as the third argument with an `id` property:

```js
const resp = await env.AI.run(
	"@cf/moonshotai/kimi-k2.5",
	{
		prompt: "tell me a joke",
	},
	{
		gateway: {
			id: "default",
		},
	},
);
```

## Gateway Options

The documentation specifies these gateway parameters:

| Parameter | Type | Purpose |
|-----------|------|---------|
| `id` | string | "Name of your AI Gateway. Must be in the same account as your Worker." |
| `skipCache` | boolean | Skip caching for the request |
| `cacheTtl` | number | Cache TTL in seconds |
| `cacheKey` | string | Custom cache key |
| `collectLog` | boolean | Whether to collect logs |
| `metadata` | object | Custom metadata for log entry |

## Streaming Support

The documentation provided does not contain information about streaming capabilities through the gateway option. The examples shown use standard request/response patterns, but streaming behavior is not explicitly addressed in this reference material.

## Approved Plan:
# WP-02 Spike: LLM shapes, streaming, `waitForApproval`, subrequests

## Context
WP-07 (LLM layer) and WP-09/12 (Workflow and approvals) depend on facts the TDD left open: the exact request and response format of the two models, whether streaming works through AI Gateway, what `waitForApproval` does when it times out, whether DO/D1 calls count toward the 50-subrequest limit, and glm's neuron rates. WP-02 answers these, records real responses as fixtures for `mock.ts`, and writes the answers into TDD §7.4 and §5.2.

## What's already known (read-only research done in planning)
- **Auth:** `wrangler whoami` shows the OAuth login, including `ai (write)`.
- **Model formats (from generated runtime types in `worker-configuration.d.ts`):**
  - gpt-oss-120b accepts **either** the OpenAI Responses format or chat completions (`messages`, `tools`, `reasoning_effort`). Its output is `ResponsesOutput | ChatCompletionsOutput`.
  - glm-4.7-flash accepts chat completions only, with `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking`, which defaults to true. Its output is `ChatCompletionsOutput`.
  - So one chat-completions mapper may serve both models. The spike confirms this at runtime.
- **AI Gateway:** docs say "If the gateway does not exist, AI Gateway creates it on the first authenticated request." So `gateway: { id: "incident-copilot" }` should just work, with no dashboard step.
- **Neuron rates (pricing page):**
  - gpt-oss-120b: 31,818 neurons per M input tokens and 68,182 per M output.
  - glm-4.7-flash: **5,500 per M input and 36,400 per M output**.
  - whisper: 46.63 per minute.
- **`waitForApproval` (agents 0.28.1 `dist/workflows.js`):** it's `step.waitForEvent(stepName, {type:"approval", timeout})`. If `approved` is false it calls `step.reportError` and then throws `WorkflowRejectedError`. The timeout behaviour comes from Workflows' `waitForEvent` and isn't documented, so the spike tests it.
- **Subrequests (docs):** Free plan allows 50 per invocation, plus a separate limit of 1,000 "subrequests to internal services". It isn't clear whether DO RPC and D1 calls count toward the 50.
- **User decisions:** no live deploy, so the subrequest question is answered from the docs and the cautious budget of 45 (counting everything) stays. Spike code is thrown away; only fixtures and notes are committed.

## Steps
1. **Board:** mark WP-02 🔄 in TASKS.md, update "Now" and log the prompt in PROMPTS.md.
2. **Throwaway spike Worker in `spike/`**, excluded with `.git/info/exclude` (no .gitignore change) and deleted at the end. It uses the repo's `node_modules`.
   - `spike/wrangler.jsonc`: name `incident-copilot-spike`, `ai` binding, a `SpikeAgent` DO (`new_sqlite_classes`), a `SPIKE_WORKFLOW` workflow (`SpikeWorkflow extends AgentWorkflow`), and the `nodejs_compat` flag.
   - `spike/index.ts` routes:
     - `GET /llm?model=gpt|glm&case=text|tools|followup|stream|responses`: calls `env.AI.run(model, input, { gateway: { id: "incident-copilot" } })` and returns the raw JSON. For `stream` it returns the raw SSE text.
     - `POST /approval/start?timeout=10%20seconds`: `agent.runWorkflow(...)`. The workflow wraps `waitForApproval` in try/catch and records the outcome (`approved` / the error's `name`+`message`) through an Agent RPC inside a `step.do`, then continues to a final step.
     - `POST /approval/approve|reject?id=`, and `GET /approval/status?id=` (the recorded outcome plus `getWorkflow` status).
   - Run it with `pnpm exec wrangler dev -c spike/wrangler.jsonc` in the background. The AI binding is remote even in dev.
3. **LLM calls (about 12 live calls, roughly 100 neurons in total):**
   - For each model: plain text; tools using the TDD `dns_lookup` and `submit_findings` schemas (shape of the tool call, args as string or object, ids); a follow-up turn with a `role:"tool"` result that should lead to a `submit_findings` call; and `stream: true` (SSE chunk format, where usage arrives, and whether text and tool calls stream).
   - For gpt only: one call in the Responses format, to compare and pick the format.
   - For glm only: one call with `enable_thinking:false`, to compare latency and tokens.
   - Record: `usage` fields (for `usage.ts`), the reasoning or "thinking" field, the error shape for a bad model ID, and whether the gateway was used (it now exists, with log id or headers).
4. **Approval checks (local workflows):** timeout fires → is it catchable in `run()`, and what are the error name and message; approve → metadata returned; reject → `WorkflowRejectedError`; `approveWorkflow` after the timeout → harmless or throws (this matters for D13's idempotent Approve).
5. **Fixtures:** save trimmed real responses to `test/fixtures/llm/<model>/{text,tool-call,tool-followup,stream.sse}.json` and add `test/fixtures/README.md` saying where they came from. Remove account IDs and gateway log IDs.
6. **TDD notes (the done-when):**
   - **§7.4:** the chosen request format (chat completions for both, unless the spike shows a reason not to), the normalised mapping into `LlmResult`, the stream format, glm thinking, the gateway auto-create, and the confirmed neuron rates (gpt 0.0318/0.0682 per token; glm 0.0055/0.0364).
   - **§5.2 `SUBREQUEST_BUDGET`:** the docs-based answer, "not verified live (user choice); keep 45 counting every fetch and RPC".
   - **§8.2 / §8.3:** adjust if the timeout or after-timeout approve behaviour differs from what's written.
   - Fold the decisions into the TASKS decisions log.
7. **Cleanup and close:**
   - Stop the dev server, delete `spike/` and remove the exclude line.
   - Run `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test`.
   - Commit `WP-02: spike findings + LLM fixtures`, then push.
   - Mark WP-02 ✅ with the hash and hours, set Next = WP-03, and commit and push the board.

## Files touched
`TDD.md` (§5.2, §7.4, maybe §8.2/§8.3), `TASKS.md`, `PROMPTS.md`, and new `test/fixtures/llm/**` and `test/fixtures/README.md`. `spike/` is temporary and never committed.

## Verification
- Every checklist item in WP-02 has a recorded answer, either from a live call or from cited docs for subrequests.
- The fixture files are valid JSON and contain no account or log IDs (`grep` for the account ID).
- `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` are still green, and `git status` shows no `spike/` files.

```

#### `runWorkflow(workflowName, params, options?)`

Start a workflow and track it in the originating Agent's database.

```typescript
const instanceId = await this.runWorkflow(
  "MY_WORKFLOW",
  { taskId: "123", data: "process this" },
  {
    id: "custom-id", // optional - auto-generated if not provided
    metadata: { userId: "user-456", priority: "high" }, // optional - for querying
    agentBinding: "MyAgent", // optional - auto-detected from class name if not provided
    retention: {
      successRetention: "1 day",
      errorRetention: "7 days"
    }
  }
);
```

**Parameters:**

- `workflowName` - Workflow binding name from `env`
- `params` - Params to pass to the workflow
- `options.id` - Custom workflow ID (auto-generated if not provided)
- `options.metadata` - Optional metadata stored for querying (not passed to workflow)
- `options.agentBinding` - Agent binding name (auto-detected from class name if not provided). When called from a sub-agent, this is the root Agent binding name.
- [`options.retention`](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workflows/build/workers-api/#workflowinstancecreateoptions) - Workflow retention passed unchanged to
  `Workflow.create()`.
  Use `successRetention` for successful instances and `errorRetention` for
  errored or terminated instances.

**Returns:** Workflow instance ID

#### Sub-agents

Sub-agents can call `this.runWorkflow()` directly. The workflow is tracked in the originating sub-agent's SQLite database, and `this.agent` inside `AgentWorkflow` routes back to that same sub-agent for RPC calls, callbacks, state updates, and broadcasts.

Always finish by calling submit_findings, citing evidence ids like [dns-1].\";\n\nasync function readStream(s: ReadableStream): Promise<string> {\n  return await new Response(s).text();\n}\n\nasync function run(env: Env, model: string, input: any) {\n  const t = Date.now();\n  try {\n    const r = await env.AI.run(model, input, GW);\n    const result = r instanceof ReadableStream ? await readStream(r) : r;\n    return { ms: Date.now() - t, logId: env.AI.aiGatewayLogId ?? null, result };\n  } catch (e: any) {\n    return { ms: Date.now() - t, error: { name: e?.name, message: e?.message, ctor: e?.constructor?.name } };\n  }\n}\n\nasync function llm(env: Env, modelKey: string, kase: string, effort?: string) {\n  const model = MODELS[modelKey]!;\n  const extra: any = {};\n  if (effort && modelKey === \"gpt\") extra.reasoning_effort = effort;\n  if (kase === \"nothink\") extra.chat_template_kwargs = { enable_thinking: false };\n  switch (kase) {\n    case \"text\":\n    case \"nothink\":\n      return run(env, model, { messages: [{ role: \"system\", content: \"Answer briefly.\" }, { role: \"user\", content: \"In one sentence, what does HTTP 502 mean?\" }], max_tokens: 400, ...extra });\n    case \"stream\":\n      return run(env, model, { messages: [{ role: \"system\", content: \"Answer briefly.\" }, { role: \"user\", content: \"In one sentence, what does HTTP 502 mean?\" }], max_tokens: 400, stream: true, ...extra });\n    case \"tools\":\n      return run(env, model, { messages: [{ role: \"system\", content: SYS }, { role: \"user\", content: \"Is example.com resolving? Check its A records.\" }], tools: TOOLS, max_tokens: 1024, ...extra });\n    case \"stream-tools\":\n      return run(env, model, { messages: [{ role: \"system\", content: SYS }, { role: \"user\", content: \"Is example.com resolving? Check its A records.\" }], tools: TOOLS, max_tokens: 1024, stream: true, ...extra });\n    case \"followup\": {\n      const first: any = await run(env, model, { messages: [{ role: \"system\", content: SYS }, { role: \"user\", content: \"Is example.com resolving? Check its A records.\" }], tools: TOOLS, max_tokens: 1024, ...extra });\n      const msg = first.result?.choices?.[0]?.message;\n      const call = msg?.tool_calls?.[0];\n      if (!call) return { first, note: \"no tool call in first turn\" };\n      const messages = [\n        { role: \"system\", content: SYS },\n        { role: \"user\", content: \"Is example.com resolving? Check its A records.\" },\n        { role: \"assistant\", content: msg.content ?? \"\", tool_calls: msg.tool_calls },\n        { role: \"tool\", tool_call_id: call.id, content: \"[dns-1] dns_lookup: 1.1.1.1 NOERROR A 93.184.215.14 ttl=300; 8.8.8.8 NOERROR A 93.184.215.14 ttl=289; mismatch=false\" },\n      ];\n      const second = await run(env, model, { messages, tools: TOOLS, max_tokens: 1024, ...extra });\n      return { first, second };\n    }\n    case \"responses\":\n      return run(env, model, { instructions: SYS, input: \"Is example.com resolving? Check its A records.\",\n        tools: TOOLS.map((t) => ({ type: \"function\", ...t.function })), max_output_tokens: 1024, ...(effort ? { reasoning: { effort } } : {}) });\n    default:\n      return { error: \"unknown case\" };\n  }\n}\n\nexport class SpikeAgent extends Agent<Env> {\n  async start(timeout: string) { return this.runWorkflow(\"SPIKE_WORKFLOW\", { timeout }); }\n  async record(id: string, outcome: unknown) { await this.ctx.storage.put(\"o:\" + id, outcome); }\n  async status(id: string) { return { outcome: (await this.ctx.storage.get(\"o:\" + id)) ?? null, info: this.getWorkflow(id) ?? null }; }\n  async approve(id: string) { try { await this.approveWorkflow(id, { reason: \"spike\", metadata: { by: \"spike\" } }); return \"ok\"; } catch (e: any) { return { name: e?.name, message: e?.message }; } }\n  async reject(id: string) { try { await this.rejectWorkflow(id, { reason: \"spike-reject\" }); return \"ok\"; } catch (e: any) { return { name: e?.name, message: e?.message }; } }\n}\n\nexport class SpikeWorkflow extends AgentWorkflow<SpikeAgent, { timeout: string }> {\n  async run(event: AgentWorkflowEvent<{ timeout: string }>, step: AgentWorkflowStep) {\n    const t0 = await step.do(\"t0\", async () => Date.now());\n    let outcome: any;\n    try {\n      const meta = await this.waitForApproval(step, { timeout: event.payload.timeout as any });\n      outcome = { result: \"approved\", meta };\n    } catch (e: any) {\n      outcome = { result: \"threw\", name: e?.name, message: e?.message, ctor: e?.constructor?.name };\n    }\n    await step.do(\"record\", async () => {\n      await this.agent.record(this.instanceId, { ...outcome, waitedMs: Date.now() - t0 });\n      return true;\n    });\n    return outcome;\n  }\n}\n\nexport default {\n  async fetch(req: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {\n    const u = new URL(req.url);\n    const q = (k: string) => u.searchParams.get(k) ?? \"\";\n    const agent = await getAgentByName(env.SpikeAgent, \"spike\");\n    let out: unknown;\n    if (u.pathname === \"/llm\") out = await llm(env, q(\"model\"), q(\"case\"), q(\"effort\") || undefined);\n    else if (u.pathname === \"/approval/start\") out = { id: await agent.start(q(\"timeout\") || \"10 seconds\") };\n    else if (u.pathname === \"/approval/status\") out = await agent.status(q(\"id\"));\n    else if (u.pathname === \"/approval/approve\") out = await agent.approve(q(\"id\"));\n    else if (u.pathname === \"/approval/reject\") out = await agent.reject(q(\"id\"));\n    else return new Response(\"not found\", { status: 404 });\n    return Response.json(out);\n  },\n};\nEOF\ngit status --short",

{

  "old_string": "- Models are called through `env.AI.run(model, input, { gateway: { id: env.AI_GATEWAY_ID } })`. `models.ts` holds the per-model request and response mappers. **The exact gpt-oss-120b input and tool-call shape is fixed in spike WP-02.**\n- After each call, `usage.ts` adds to the neuron estimate: gpt-oss-120b uses ≈ 0.032 neurons per input token and 0.068 per output token, and the glm rates are to be confirmed in WP-02.\n- A Workers AI \"daily allocation exceeded\" error sets the day to `NEURON_DAILY`.\n- When `LLM_MODE=mock`, `mock.ts` returns scripted turns keyed by target pattern (demo modes, `cloudflare.com`, generic) and streams canned text.",
  "new_string": "- Models are called through `env.AI.run(model, input, { gateway: { id: env.AI_GATEWAY_ID } })`. Both models use the **OpenAI chat-completions format**, so `client.ts` has one request builder and one response mapper. `models.ts` only adds per-model extras (below). Shapes were confirmed in spike WP-02.\n- After each call, `usage.ts` adds **`usage.neurons`**, which Workers AI reports on every response. If it's missing, it falls back to the token rates: gpt-oss-120b 31,818 / 68,182 neurons per M input/output tokens (≈ 0.0318 / 0.0682 per token), and glm-4.7-flash 5,500 / 36,400 per M (≈ 0.0055 / 0.0364).\n- A Workers AI \"daily allocation exceeded\" error sets the day to `NEURON_DAILY`.\n- When `LLM_MODE=mock`, `mock.ts` returns scripted turns keyed by target pattern (demo modes, `cloudflare.com`, generic) and streams canned text. Its responses are built from the real recorded responses in `test/fixtures/llm/`.\n\n#### 7.4.1 Spike findings (WP-02, 2026-10-15, live calls from `wrangler dev`)\n\n| Topic | Finding | Consequence for the build |\n|---|---|---|\n| Request | `{messages, tools, max_tokens, tool_choice?, stream?}`. Tools use the OpenAI shape `{type:\"function\", function:{name, description, parameters}}`. A tool result goes back as `{role:\"tool\", tool_call_id, content}` after the assistant message that holds `tool_calls`. | One mapper for both models. gpt-oss-120b also accepts the Responses format, but it rejected these tool definitions and offers nothing extra, so it isn't used. |\n| Response | `choices[0].message.{content, tool_calls[], reasoning, reasoning_content}` and `finish_reason` (`tool_calls` or `stop`). `tool_calls[i] = {id, type:\"function\", function:{name, arguments}}`, where **`arguments` is a JSON string**. | `JSON.parse` the arguments, then validate with zod. A parse failure counts as an invalid call (FR-12). `reasoning*` is **never** shown, stored or sent back to the model. |\n| `submit_findings` | With the default `tool_choice`, gpt answered in prose after the tool result and **didn't** call `submit_findings`. glm called it, but put whole sentences in `evidence[]`. `tool_choice:\"required\"` made gpt call it. Naming the tool in `tool_choice` was ignored. | Planning turns send **`tool_choice:\"required\"`**. The schema says `evidence` holds IDs only (`dns-1`); zod accepts `[dns-1]` or `dns-1` and rejects anything else. Prose `content` on a tool turn is ignored. |\n| glm thinking | On by default. With `chat_template_kwargs:{enable_thinking:false}` it takes about 2 s. | glm is **always** called with thinking off. |\n| gpt reasoning | Can't be turned off; `reasoning_effort` low/medium/high (default medium). LLM steps have the model fallback inside the step. `finishRun` runs before the approval wait, so the input is enabled again while waiting (D13).\n\n**`waitForApproval` behaviour (WP-02, local Workflows, `agents` 0.28.1):**\n- **Approve:** returns the `metadata` passed to `approveWorkflow`.\n- **Reject:** throws `WorkflowRejectedError` (\"Workflow rejected: <reason>\").\n- **Timeout:** throws a plain `Error` (\"Execution timed out after <ms> ms\"). It can be caught in `run()`, and the Workflow carries on to later steps. The catch rule is: `WorkflowRejectedError` → rejected, **any other error from the wait → expired**. Don't match on the message text, which may differ in production.\n- **Late or repeated calls:** calling `approveWorkflow` after the timeout, or a second time, doesn't throw. D13's idempotency is still enforced through the `approvals` table.\n- **The SDK's built-in tracking (`getWorkflow`) isn't a source of truth:** a rejection calls `step.reportError` and shows `errored` even though the run continues, and other runs stay `queued` unless `step.reportComplete` is called. The UI reads the `approvals` and `runs` tables only.\n- Inside `AgentWorkflow.run()`, the instance ID is **`this.workflowId`** (or `event.instanceId`). The documented `this.instanceId` is `undefined` in 0.28.1."
}

### 🤖 Claude · 01:11:02

WP-02 is done and pushed in `a030286` (findings and recorded responses) and `2064de4` (board). It took about 0.25 h against the 0.75 h estimate and used roughly 250 of the 10,000 free daily neurons.

**What the spike found:**
- **One request format fits both models.** gpt-oss-120b and glm-4.7-flash both use the standard OpenAI chat format, and streaming works through the gateway. One adapter in WP-07 can serve both models.
- **Each response reports its own cost** (`usage.neurons`), and the numbers match the published rates. The budget counter can add these up instead of estimating.
- **gpt skipped `submit_findings`.** Without being forced, it answered in prose and never made the final structured call, which the design depends on. Sending `tool_choice: "required"` fixed it, but that turn took 19 s once, so latency needs watching in WP-09. glm did make the call, but put sentences where evidence IDs belong; tighter validation will handle that.
- **glm must run with "thinking" off.** With it on, calls took 12–21 s and one came back empty. With it off they take about 2 s.
- **Approval timeouts are safe.** When the wait times out, it raises an error the Workflow can catch, and the run carries on. Approving late or twice causes no error.
- **Two Agents SDK quirks for later WPs:** the documented `this.instanceId` doesn't exist (use `this.workflowId`), and the SDK's built-in Workflow status can show "errored" after a rejection even though the run continued. Our own tables have to be what the app relies on.
- **Subrequest limit: not settled.** The docs are ambiguous, and you chose not to test it live, so the budget stays at a cautious 45 that counts every call.

All of this is written into TDD §5.2, §7.4.1, §8.2 and the risks table, and logged in the TASKS decisions log.

**Recorded responses:** 12 files in `test/fixtures/llm/`, with a README explaining each one, for WP-07's mock and adapter tests. They contain no account or log IDs.

**Spike code:** deleted as agreed. It was never committed. Typecheck, lint and tests are still green.

**Gateway:** `incident-copilot` works now that you've created it, and the TDD records that it was set up by hand in the dashboard.

Next is WP-03: the database, the workspace cookie and the target parser.

190:| `http_probe` | `url`, `method` ∈ {GET, HEAD} | 3 samples: status, time to first byte, total time; redirect hops (recorded, not followed beyond 5); selected headers (`server`, `cf-ray`, `cf-cache-status`, `cache-control`, `content-type`, `location`, `set-cookie` present?); first ≤ 16 KB of body as an **untrusted snippet** (≤ 500 chars shown to the LLM) | Distinguishes slow vs. flaky vs. down; detects redirect loops and cache misconfiguration. |
226:  W -- read/write --> D1[(D1<br/>workspaces · incidents_index<br/>watches · webhooks · usage)]
232:  DW -- admin API via Service Binding --> DEMO[Demo service Worker<br/>per-workspace modes]
241:| **Main Worker** | Serves the React app (Static Assets), API routes, workspace cookie, rate limiting, voice transcription, cron handler. |
245:| **D1** | Incident index per workspace, watch job table, encrypted webhooks, daily LLM usage counter. |
246:| **Demo service Worker** | Public demo endpoint with per-workspace modes; admin API reachable only through a Service Binding. |
272:- `incident` — id, workspace_id, target, status, category, confidence, created_at, updated_at
281:workspaces      (id TEXT PK, created_at INTEGER)
282:incidents_index (id TEXT PK, workspace_id TEXT, target TEXT, status TEXT,
284:watches         (id TEXT PK, workspace_id TEXT, url TEXT, interval_sec INTEGER,
287:webhooks        (workspace_id TEXT PK, kind TEXT, url_ciphertext BLOB, iv BLOB, created_at INTEGER)
289:usage_counters  (workspace_id TEXT, window_start INTEGER, kind TEXT,  -- kind: diagnosis | voice
290:                 count INTEGER, PRIMARY KEY (workspace_id, kind, window_start))
303:| LLM cost / quota exhaustion | **Burst:** 3 diagnoses a minute per workspace via the Rate Limiting binding (it only supports 10 s or 60 s windows). **Sustained:** 10 diagnoses an hour per workspace via a **D1 `usage_counters`** fixed-window counter. Plus AI Gateway rate limits and the daily budget guard (§6.5). |

### 🤖 Claude · 01:13:32

Hono can set and read `__Host-` cookies directly, and the test plugin can load D1 migrations into tests, so nothing extra needs installing. Writing the plan.
