The chat flow (D9) also needs a deterministic parser that turns the first message into a target. WP-03 delivers these. Done when: a cookie is set, `/api/session` returns `demoUrl`, and the target unit tests pass.\n\n## Decisions already made (no new questions)\n- D1 is created with **`wrangler d1 create incident-copilot`** (the WP-01 decision). Terraform imports it later. Migrations are applied **locally** now; the remote apply happens at the first deploy (WP-13) and then in CI (WP-15).\n- `wrangler.jsonc` gains only the bindings WP-03 uses: `d1_databases` (DB) and `version_metadata` (for `/api/health` `version`). Run `pnpm cf-typegen` afterwards.\n\n## Steps\n1. **Board:** WP-03 🔄 in TASKS.md, update \"Now\", log the prompt in PROMPTS.md.\n2. **D1:** run `pnpm exec wrangler d1 create incident-copilot` and put the `database_id` into `wrangler.jsonc` (`binding: \"DB\"`, `migrations_dir: \"migrations\"`). Write `migrations/0001_init.sql` **verbatim from TDD §6.1** (all six tables and both indexes). Apply locally with `wrangler d1 migrations apply DB --local`.\n3. **`worker/lib/ids.ts`:** `newId()` gives 128-bit random hex (32 chars, `crypto.getRandomValues`), per TDD §6.4. `isId(s)` checks `/^[0-9a-f]{32}$/`.\n4. **`worker/lib/cookie.ts` + workspace middleware** (in `worker/api/router.ts`):\n   - Read with Hono `getCookie(c, \"ws\", \"host\")` (`__Host-ws`).\n   - If the cookie is missing or not a valid ID, mint a new one, `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO workspaces`, and `setCookie(c, \"ws\", id, { prefix: \"host\", httpOnly: true, secure: true, sameSite: \"Lax\", path: \"/\", maxAge: 31536000 })`.\n   - A valid cookie is trusted **without a D1 read**, so there's no per-request DB cost. A forged ID just behaves like a new workspace, which is harmless.\n   - `c.set(\"workspaceId\", id)`. Applies to `/api/*` **except `/api/health`**, which never sets a cookie (smoke test).\n5. **`worker/api/router.ts`:** the Hono app with the middleware, mounting `health`, `session`, and the JSON 404. `worker/index.ts` delegates to it, with a comment for where `routeAgentRequest` goes in WP-08. Extend `AppEnv` with `Variables: { workspaceId: string }`.\n6. **`/api/health`** → `{ ok: true, version: env.CF_VERSION_METADATA.id }`.\n7. **`/api/session`** → `{ demoUrl, budgetMode, limits }`:\n   - `demoUrl` = `${DEMO_BASE_URL}/w/${workspaceId}/` (trailing slash handled).\n   - `budgetMode: \"ai\"` for now; WP-17 connects it to the guard. There's a comment saying so.\n   - `limits: { runsPerMinute: 3, runsPerHour: 10, voicePerHour: 20, watchesPerWorkspace: 3 }`, from a small `worker/lib/limits.ts` that WP-17/18 reuse.\n8. **`worker/safety/target.ts`**, a pure function `parseTarget(message, demoUrl)` → `{ ok: true, url, host, source: \"url\"|\"host\"|\"demo\" } | { ok: false, error: \"need_target\" }`:\n   - **Normalise first:** `<word> dot <word>` → `<word>.<word>` (case-insensitive, repeated), so \"demo dot example dot com\" becomes \"demo.example.com\".\n   - **1. URL:** the first `https?://…` token. Trailing `.,;:!?)]'\"` is trimmed, then `new URL()`. Other schemes such as `ftp://` aren't taken as a URL, so the message falls through to the host rule.\n   - **2. Host token:** the first `label(.label)+` with a 2–63-letter TLD, skipping common file extensions (`html js json css php txt md ts png jpg`). Also IPv4 literals and the bare word `localhost`, so that WP-04's SSRF guard can refuse them with a clear message (PRD §13). Lowercased, trailing dot dropped, `url = https://<host>/`.\n   - **3. Demo:** the whole word \"demo\" (case-insensitive) → `demoUrl`.\n   - **Otherwise:** `need_target`.\n   - The parser does **not** do any safety checks; that's WP-04 (`ssrf.ts`).\n9. **Tests:**\n   - `test/unit/target.test.ts`, a table covering:\n     - URL vs host vs demo precedence (\"check demo.example.com\" → host, \"my demo is slow\" → demo);\n     - \"dot\" normalisation;\n     - trailing punctuation, uppercase, ports and paths;\n     - `e.g.` and `index.html` ignored;\n     - `localhost` and `169.254.169.254` extracted;\n     - `ftp://x.com` → host `x.com`;\n     - empty or no-target text → `need_target`.\n   - `test/integration/session.test.ts`:\n     - no cookie → `Set-Cookie: __Host-ws=<32 hex>; …HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Path=/; Max-Age=31536000`, `demoUrl` ends with `/w/<id>/`, and a `workspaces` row exists;\n     - with that cookie → no new `Set-Cookie` and the same `demoUrl`;\n     - an invalid cookie → a new ID;\n     - `/api/health` → `{ok, version}` with no `Set-Cookie`.\n   - D1 in tests: `vitest.config.ts` (integration project) reads migrations with `readD1Migrations(\"./migrations\")` into a `TEST_MIGRATIONS` binding, and `test/integration/setup.ts` runs `applyD1Migrations(env.DB, env.TEST_MIGRATIONS)`.\n10. **Close:**\n    - Run `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test`.\n    - Manual check: `pnpm dev`, `curl -i /api/session` (cookie plus `demoUrl`).\n    - Commit `WP-03: D1 schema, workspace cookie, session/health, target parser`, push, set ✅ on the board with the hash and hours, then commit and push the board.\n\n## Notes / risks carried forward\n- `DEMO_BASE_URL` still contains `<sub>` because the workers.dev subdomain is only known at the first deploy (WP-13). `demoUrl` is correct apart from that host.\n- **For WP-11:** locally, the demo service runs only as an auxiliary Worker (no public URL), and the SSRF guard will block `localhost`. Decide in WP-11 how local probes reach the demo (for example, a dev-only probe through the `DEMO` binding).\n- Safari doesn't keep `Secure`/`__Host-` cookies on `http://localhost`. Use Chrome or Firefox for local UI work.\n\n## Files\n`wrangler.jsonc`, `migrations/0001_init.sql`, `worker/index.ts`, `worker/env.ts`, `worker/api/{router,session,health}.ts`, `worker/lib/{ids,cookie,limits}.ts`, `worker/safety/target.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`, `test/integration/{setup,session}.test.ts`, `test/unit/target.test.ts`, `worker-configuration.d.ts` (regenerated), `TASKS.md`, `PROMPTS.md`.\n\n## Verification\n- `pnpm test`: target table, session/cookie and health tests pass alongside the existing 6.\n- `curl -i localhost:5173/api/session` once without a cookie (Set-Cookie present) and once with it (absent, same `demoUrl`).\n- `wrangler d1 execute DB --local --command \"select count(*) from workspaces\"` increases by one per new visitor.\n"
}

## Approved Plan:
# WP-03 D1 and workspace: migration, cookie, router, session/health, target parser

## Context
Every later WP needs an anonymous workspace identity (FR-8, D7, D8) and the D1 schema (TDD §6.1). The chat flow (D9) also needs a deterministic parser that turns the first message into a target. WP-03 delivers these. Done when: a cookie is set, `/api/session` returns `demoUrl`, and the target unit tests pass.

## Decisions already made (no new questions)
- D1 is created with **`wrangler d1 create incident-copilot`** (the WP-01 decision). Terraform imports it later. Migrations are applied **locally** now; the remote apply happens at the first deploy (WP-13) and then in CI (WP-15).
- `wrangler.jsonc` gains only the bindings WP-03 uses: `d1_databases` (DB) and `version_metadata` (for `/api/health` `version`). Run `pnpm cf-typegen` afterwards.

## Steps
1. **Board:** WP-03 🔄 in TASKS.md, update "Now", log the prompt in PROMPTS.md.
2. **D1:** run `pnpm exec wrangler d1 create incident-copilot` and put the `database_id` into `wrangler.jsonc` (`binding: "DB"`, `migrations_dir: "migrations"`). Write `migrations/0001_init.sql` **verbatim from TDD §6.1** (all six tables and both indexes). Apply locally with `wrangler d1 migrations apply DB --local`.
3. **`worker/lib/ids.ts`:** `newId()` gives 128-bit random hex (32 chars, `crypto.getRandomValues`), per TDD §6.4. `isId(s)` checks `/^[0-9a-f]{32}$/`.
4. **`worker/lib/cookie.ts` + workspace middleware** (in `worker/api/router.ts`):
   - Read with Hono `getCookie(c, "ws", "host")` (`__Host-ws`).
   - If the cookie is missing or not a valid ID, mint a new one, `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO workspaces`, and `setCookie(c, "ws", id, { prefix: "host", httpOnly: true, secure: true, sameSite: "Lax", path: "/", maxAge: 31536000 })`.
   - A valid cookie is trusted **without a D1 read**, so there's no per-request DB cost. A forged ID just behaves like a new workspace, which is harmless.
   - `c.set("workspaceId", id)`. Applies to `/api/*` **except `/api/health`**, which never sets a cookie (smoke test).
5. **`worker/api/router.ts`:** the Hono app with the middleware, mounting `health`, `session`, and the JSON 404. `worker/index.ts` delegates to it, with a comment for where `routeAgentRequest` goes in WP-08. Extend `AppEnv` with `Variables: { workspaceId: string }`.
6. **`/api/health`** → `{ ok: true, version: env.CF_VERSION_METADATA.id }`.
7. **`/api/session`** → `{ demoUrl, budgetMode, limits }`:
   - `demoUrl` = `${DEMO_BASE_URL}/w/${workspaceId}/` (trailing slash handled).
   - `budgetMode: "ai"` for now; WP-17 connects it to the guard. There's a comment saying so.
   - `limits: { runsPerMinute: 3, runsPerHour: 10, voicePerHour: 20, watchesPerWorkspace: 3 }`, from a small `worker/lib/limits.ts` that WP-17/18 reuse.
8. **`worker/safety/target.ts`**, a pure function `parseTarget(message, demoUrl)` → `{ ok: true, url, host, source: "url"|"host"|"demo" } | { ok: false, error: "need_target" }`:
   - **Normalise first:** `<word> dot <word>` → `<word>.<word>` (case-insensitive, repeated), so "demo dot example dot com" becomes "demo.example.com".
   - **1. URL:** the first `https?://…` token. Trailing `.,;:!?)]'"` is trimmed, then `new URL()`. Other schemes such as `ftp://` aren't taken as a URL, so the message falls through to the host rule.
   - **2. Host token:** the first `label(.label)+` with a 2–63-letter TLD, skipping common file extensions (`html js json css php txt md ts png jpg`). Also IPv4 literals and the bare word `localhost`, so that WP-04's SSRF guard can refuse them with a clear message (PRD §13). Lowercased, trailing dot dropped, `url = https://<host>/`.
   - **3. Demo:** the whole word "demo" (case-insensitive) → `demoUrl`.
   - **Otherwise:** `need_target`.
   - The parser does **not** do any safety checks; that's WP-04 (`ssrf.ts`).
9. **Tests:**
   - `test/unit/target.test.ts`, a table covering:
     - URL vs host vs demo precedence ("check demo.example.com" → host, "my demo is slow" → demo);
     - "dot" normalisation;
     - trailing punctuation, uppercase, ports and paths;
     - `e.g.` and `index.html` ignored;
     - `localhost` and `169.254.169.254` extracted;
     - `ftp://x.com` → host `x.com`;
     - empty or no-target text → `need_target`.
   - `test/integration/session.test.ts`:
     - no cookie → `Set-Cookie: __Host-ws=<32 hex>; …HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Path=/; Max-Age=31536000`, `demoUrl` ends with `/w/<id>/`, and a `workspaces` row exists;
     - with that cookie → no new `Set-Cookie` and the same `demoUrl`;
     - an invalid cookie → a new ID;
     - `/api/health` → `{ok, version}` with no `Set-Cookie`.
   - D1 in tests: `vitest.config.ts` (integration project) reads migrations with `readD1Migrations("./migrations")` into a `TEST_MIGRATIONS` binding, and `test/integration/setup.ts` runs `applyD1Migrations(env.DB, env.TEST_MIGRATIONS)`.
10. **Close:**
    - Run `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test`.
    - Manual check: `pnpm dev`, `curl -i /api/session` (cookie plus `demoUrl`).
    - Commit `WP-03: D1 schema, workspace cookie, session/health, target parser`, push, set ✅ on the board with the hash and hours, then commit and push the board.

## Notes / risks carried forward
- `DEMO_BASE_URL` still contains `<sub>` because the workers.dev subdomain is only known at the first deploy (WP-13). `demoUrl` is correct apart from that host.
- **For WP-11:** locally, the demo service runs only as an auxiliary Worker (no public URL), and the SSRF guard will block `localhost`. Decide in WP-11 how local probes reach the demo (for example, a dev-only probe through the `DEMO` binding).
- Safari doesn't keep `Secure`/`__Host-` cookies on `http://localhost`. Use Chrome or Firefox for local UI work.

## Files

/\\b((?:\\d{1,3}\\.){3}\\d{1,3}|localhost|(?:[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9-]{0,61}[a-z0-9])?\\.)+[a-z]{2,63})\\.?(?![a-z0-9-])/gi;\nconst FILE_EXTENSIONS = new Set([\n  \"html\", \"htm\", \"js\", \"mjs\", keyword last\n    [\"what's wrong with my demo service?\", DEMO, \"incident-copilot-demo.example.workers.dev\", \"demo\"],\n    [\"DEMO is slow\", DEMO, \"incident-copilot-demo.example.workers.dev\", ### 🤖 Claude · 01:19:13

WP-03 is done and pushed (`9d691c6`, board `068172b`). It took about 0.25 h against the 0.75 h estimate.

**What's in place:**
- **Database:** I created the D1 database `incident-copilot` on your Cloudflare account (region APAC). All six tables from TDD §6.1 are applied locally. The live database gets them at the first deploy (WP-13), and from CI after that.
- **Workspace cookie:** the first visit to any `/api/*` route creates an anonymous workspace and sets a `__Host-ws` cookie that lasts a year. Later visits reuse it without a database lookup. `/api/health` never sets the cookie, so health checks don't create workspaces.
- **`/api/session`:** returns the demo URL for your workspace and the usage limits. Two placeholders remain: the budget status reads `"ai"` until WP-17, and the demo URL contains `<sub>` until the first deploy tells us your workers.dev subdomain.
- **`/api/health`:** now also returns a version ID.
- **Target parser:** finds what to diagnose in the first message. It checks for a URL first, then a hostname, then the word "demo", and turns spoken "dot" into ".". It deliberately picks up `localhost` and private IPs so WP-04's safety check can refuse them with a clear message. 25 test cases cover it.

**Checks:** typecheck, lint and all 35 tests pass. The cookie, session and health tests run against a real local database. I also checked by hand against the dev server: the first request set the cookie, the second reused it, and the workspace count went from 0 to 2, one per cookie-less visit.

**Two things I fixed along the way:**
- Unit tests for Worker code and for browser code need different TypeScript settings, so browser tests now live in `test/unit/web/`. This is in the decisions log.
- My dev server from WP-01 had been running since 00:35 because my earlier stop command didn't catch it. I've stopped it.

**Open question for WP-11 (on the board):** when running locally, the demo service has no public URL, and the safety check will block `localhost`. We'll need to decide how local probes reach the demo.

Next is WP-04, the safety core: the private-address (SSRF) guard, capped reads, the origin pin and the call budget.

**How it will work:** a dev-only setting, `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN`, lets exactly one origin (scheme, host and port) through the safety check. It's set only in your local `.dev.vars` file and never in the deployed config. In production nothing is exempt, so private and `localhost` addresses stay blocked and the demo is reached through its public workers.dev URL.

**Where it's written down:**
- **TDD §11:** describes the exception and that production never sets it.
- **WP-04 checklist:** build the exception in `ssrf.ts`, with tests for it being on, off, and a near-miss (for example a different port is still blocked).
- **WP-11 checklist:** set the local demo URL in `.dev.vars` and point the exception at it.
- **TASKS decisions log and "Now":** the open question is closed.
- **PROMPTS.md:** your message is logged.

Next is WP-04, the safety core, whenever you're ready.

### 7.3 Tool contracts (`tools/registry.ts`). Inputs use zod. Outputs are `ToolResult {evidenceId, ok, status, summary, digest, raw}`.

| Tool | Input | Digest (what the LLM sees, ≤ 1.5 KB) | Max subrequests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dns_lookup` | `{hostname, types?: ("A"\|"AAAA"\|"CNAME"\|"NS"\|"MX"\|"TXT")[] = ["A","AAAA"]}` | For each resolver (1.1.1.1 `cloudflare-dns.com/dns-query`, 8.8.8.8 `dns.google/resolve`, both DoH JSON): status, answers and TTLs, the CNAME chain, a `mismatch` flag, and `ssrf_blocked` if any answer is private | 2 × types |
| `http_probe` | `{url, method?: "GET"\|"HEAD" = "GET"}` | For each of 3 samples: status, TTFB and total ms. Also the hop list (≤ 5, `redirect: "manual"`, each hop SSRF-checked), selected headers (`server`, `cf-ray`, `cf-cache-status`, `cache-control`, `content-type`, `location`, `set-cookie` as present or absent), a `<untrusted>` snippet of ≤ 500 chars, and an `asset` sub-probe (D10) with its URL, status, `cache-control` and whether `set-cookie` was present | 3 × (1 + 5) + 1 |
| `cert_info` | `{hostname}` | Up to 3 unexpired certificates: issuer, notBefore, notAfter, daysToExpiry, `sanMatch`. Labeled "CT logs, best effort". On error: `status: "unavailable"`. | 1 |
| `provider_status` | `{providers: ("cloudflare"\|"github"\|"aws")[]}` | Active incidents per provider. Statuspage `/api/v2/incidents/unresolved.json` for Cloudflare and GitHub (small payloads). AWS health is best effort. | ≤ 3 |
| `submit_findings` | `{category, confidence, evidence: string[≥1], cause: string≤300, next_steps: string[≤4], actions: ActionId[≤1]}` | Not a check. It ends planning (D5). | 0 |

**Origin pin (D6):** `safety/origin.ts` `assertInScope(tool, args, incident)`. A failure counts as an invalid call (FR-12).

**`quickCheck(url)`** (cron, monitor ticks and verify): 1 DoH A query plus 1 GET following up to 5 hops. A pass means a final 2xx/3xx under `HIGH_LATENCY_MS`. Costs at most 7 subrequests.

## 11. Safety implementation

- **SSRF (`safety/ssrf.ts`, `iprange.ts`):**
  - Only `http` and `https` schemes, and only port 80/443 (an empty port is allowed).
  - Reject IP literals and `localhost`/`.local`/`.internal`.
  - Resolve the host with DoH before fetching and reject it if **any** A or AAAA answer is in a blocked range: 0/8, 10/8, 100.64/10, 127/8, 169.254/16, 172.16/12, 192.0.0/24, 192.0.2/24, 192.168/16, 198.18/15, 198.51.100/24, 203.0.113/24, 224/4, 240/4, ::/128, ::1, fc00::/7, fe80::/10, ff00::/8, ::ffff:0:0/96 (checked against the embedded v4 address), 64:ff9b::/96, 2001:db8::/32.
  - Apply the same check to every redirect hop.
  - **Dev-only exception:** when `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` is set, that one exact origin (scheme, host and port, for example the local demo service) skips the checks above. It's set only in `.dev.vars` for local work and never in `wrangler.jsonc` `vars`, so in production the demo is reached through its public `workers.dev` URL and everything private stays blocked. There's no wildcard and no partial match.
  - Defense in depth: `global_fetch_strictly_public`. A DNS-rebinding race can't be fully closed, and the README says so.
- **Open proxy:** GET/HEAD only. No headers, bodies or cookies from the user. `reader.ts` caps reads at 16 KB, aborts after 10 s, and never follows redirects automatically. Origin pin (D6).
- **Probe rate:** `PROBE_LIMIT.limit({key: hostname})` runs before every probe sample. If it's exceeded, the probe returns `unavailable: rate_limited`.
- **Quotas (D14):** `RUN_LIMIT.limit({key: ws})` plus the D1 fixed-window `usage_counters(kind='run')`, which uses an atomic `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET count = count + 1 RETURNING count`.
- **Budget guard:** before every run, `llm_usage(today).neurons_est ≥ 9000` → `mode = rule_based`. `GET /api/session` returns `budgetMode`, which drives the `BudgetBanner`.
- **Webhooks:**
worker/env.ts
worker/index.ts

worker:
api
env.ts
index.ts
lib
safety

worker/api:
health.ts
router.ts
session.ts

worker/lib:
cookie.ts
ids.ts
limits.ts

worker/safety:
target.ts
<summary><b>WP-04</b> Safety core</summary>

- [ ] `ssrf.ts` + `iprange.ts` (all blocked v4/v6 ranges)
- [ ] `reader.ts` (16 KB cap, 10 s abort, manual redirects)
- [ ] `origin.ts` (`assertInScope`)
- [ ] Budget / subrequest counter
- [ ] Dev-only `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN`: an exact origin match skips the guard; unset in prod (TDD §11)
- [ ] `ssrf.test.ts` (40+ cases, including the allow-origin on/off/near-miss cases), `origin.test.ts`
- [ ] **Done when:** SSRF and origin unit tables pass
</details>

  "content": "# WP-04 Safety core: SSRF guard, reader, origin pin, budget\n\n## Context\nWP-05's tools (`dns_lookup`, `http_probe`, `quickCheck`) fetch URLs that users and the LLM choose. Before any of that exists, the app needs the safety layer from TDD §11 and D6: an SSRF guard that refuses private and reserved addresses on every hop, a capped reader that never follows redirects on its own, the origin pin, and a subrequest budget (Free plan: 50 per invocation). WP-04 builds these as **pure, dependency-injected modules** (`fetch` is passed in), so they're unit-testable in node. Done when: the SSRF and origin unit tables pass.\n\n## Design\n\n### `worker/safety/iprange.ts`\n- `parseIpv4(s) → number | null` and `parseIpv6(s) → bigint | null`. The IPv6 parser handles `::` compression and an embedded dotted IPv4 tail; brackets are stripped by the caller.\n- `isBlockedIp(ip) → boolean`. It checks every TDD §11 range:\n  - **IPv4:** 0/8, 10/8, 100.64/10, 127/8, 169.254/16, 172.16/12, 192.0.0/24, 192.0.2/24, 192.168/16, 198.18/15, 198.51.100/24, 203.0.113/24, 224/4, 240/4.\n  - **IPv6:** ::/128, ::1, fc00::/7, fe80::/10, ff00::/8, 64:ff9b::/96, 2001:db8::/32.\n  - **IPv4-mapped (`::ffff:0:0/96`):** the embedded v4 address is checked against the v4 list.\n- **Fails closed:** anything that doesn't parse counts as blocked.\n\n### `worker/safety/ssrf.ts`\n1. **`checkUrlShape(url, env)`** (no network) → `{ok:true, url: URL} | {ok:false, reason}`:\n   - **Dev exception first:** if `env.SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` is set and `new URL(url).origin === it` (exact scheme, host and port), allow it.\n   - Only `http:` and `https:`.\n   - Port empty, 80 or 443.\n   - No IP literals: v4, or bracketed v6. WHATWG `URL` already turns `http://2130706433/` and `0x7f.1` into dotted v4, so those are caught too.\n   - No `localhost`, `*.localhost`, `*.local`, `*.internal`, and no single-label hosts (intranet names).\n   - Any credentials in the URL are rejected.\n   - Reasons are short codes plus readable text, for example `private_address`: \"169.254.169.254 is a private or reserved address\".\n2. **`resolveAndCheck(host, deps)`**: DoH JSON lookups for A **and** AAAA (`cloudflare-dns.com`), reusing `lib/doh.ts`:\n   - Blocked if **any** A/AAAA answer is in a blocked range (CNAME answers are skipped).\n   - `{ok:false, reason:\"no_addresses\"}` if there are none. The caller treats that as a DNS failure, not an SSRF refusal.\n3. **`createGuard(deps)`** → `guard(url)`, which runs 1 then 2 with a **per-tool-call cache** of host results. So each distinct host costs at most 2 DoH subrequests, however many samples or hops hit it.\n\n### `worker/lib/doh.ts`\n- `dohQuery(fetch, resolverUrl, name, type) → {status, answers:[{name,type,ttl,data}]}`.\n- Shared with WP-05 `dns_lookup`, which calls both resolvers.\n\n### `worker/safety/reader.ts`\n- **Constants:** `READ_CAP = 16 KB`, `FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 10 000`, `MAX_HOPS = 5`.\n- **`readOnce(fetch, url, method)`:**\n  - `redirect: \"manual\"`, `AbortSignal.timeout`, a fixed `user-agent`, and no user headers or body.\n  - Streams at most 16 KB, then cancels.\n  - Returns `{status, headers, location, body, truncated, ttfbMs, totalMs}` or `{error: \"timeout\"|\"network\", message}`.\n- **`followHops(fetch, guard, url, method)`:**\n  - Calls `guard` on **every** hop URL, then `readOnce`.\n  - Follows `Location` (relative locations are resolved) up to 5 hops.\n  - Stops on a loop (a URL seen before), the hop cap, or a guard refusal.\n  - Returns `{hops:[{url,status,location,ttfbMs,totalMs}], final, stop: \"done\"|\"loop\"|\"max_hops\"|\"blocked\"|\"error\", blockedReason?}`.\n- WP-05 builds samples, header picks and snippets on top of this.\n\n### `worker/safety/origin.ts`\n- `assertInScope(tool, args, scope)` with `scope = { host, cnameTargets: string[] }`. It throws `OutOfScopeError(reason)`, which WP-09 counts as an invalid call (FR-12).\n- **`http_probe`:** the URL's host must equal the incident host, case-insensitive. Any path is allowed.\n- **`dns_lookup` and `cert_info`:** the hostname must be the incident host or one of this incident's CNAME targets.\n- **`provider_status`:** always in scope (zod enum).\n- **Unknown tool:** throws.\n\n### `worker/loop/budget.ts`\n- The TDD §5.2 loop constants (`MAX_TOOL_CALLS`, `MAX_PLAN_TURNS`, `RUN_DEADLINE_MS`, `SUBREQUEST_BUDGET = 45`, `HTTP_SAMPLES`, …).\n- **`SubrequestBudget`:** a serialisable `{limit, used}` (it lives in the run row between Workflow steps), with `remaining()`, `reserve(n) → boolean` (the worst case is reserved before a tool runs), `settle(reserved, actual)`, and `toJSON`/`from`.\n- **`countingFetch(fetch, budget)`:** wraps `fetch`, adds 1 per call and throws `BudgetExceededError` when the limit is reached. Used for actual counting, including by cron in WP-18.\n\n### TDD fold-in (logged in TASKS)\n§7.3's maximum subrequests didn't include the guard's DNS lookups. Updated estimates:\n- **`http_probe`:** `3 × (1 + 5) + 1 + 2 × distinct hosts`.\n- **`quickCheck`:** about 8 (1 A query plus the guard's AAAA, plus 6 fetches), more if redirects go to other hosts.\n- The per-run reservation and `countingFetch` keep runs within 45 and cron within 50.\n\n## Steps\n1. **Board:** WP-04 🔄 in TASKS and \"Now\", and log the prompt in PROMPTS.\n2. Write `lib/doh.ts`, `safety/iprange.ts`, `safety/ssrf.ts`, `safety/reader.ts`, `safety/origin.ts` and `loop/budget.ts` as designed.\n3. Add `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` as an **optional** string in `worker/env.ts`, extending the generated `Env`. It's not added to `wrangler.jsonc` `vars`, and `.dev.vars.example` gets a commented line pointing to WP-11.\n4. **Tests** (`test/unit/`, node, with a fake `fetch`):\n   - **`ssrf.test.ts` (40+ cases):**\n     - an IP table: every blocked range at its edges, plus allowed public v4 and v6 (1.1.1.1, 8.8.8.8, 2606:4700::1111);\n     - mapped v4 (`::ffff:10.0.0.1` blocked, `::ffff:1.1.1.1` allowed);\n     - URL shape: schemes, ports, literals, `[::1]`, decimal and hex IPs, localhost variants, `.internal`, single-label hosts, credentials;\n     - DoH: public, any-private-in-the-mix, AAAA-only private, CNAME then public, no answers;\n     - the cache (2 DoH calls for repeated hosts);\n     - a hop re-check (a public URL redirecting to `http://169.254.169.254/` → `blocked` at hop 2);\n     - **dev allow-origin:** on (exact match allowed), off (blocked), near-misses (other port, other scheme, subdomain) blocked.\n   - **`reader.test.ts`:** 16 KB cap (`truncated`), timeout → `error: \"timeout\"`, the redirect loop, the 5-hop cap, relative `Location`, and that no redirect is ever followed automatically.\n   - **`origin.test.ts`:** the host matches with any path, another host is refused, CNAME targets are allowed for `dns_lookup`/`cert_info` but not for `http_probe`, case-insensitivity, and an unknown tool.\n   - **`budget.test.ts`:** reserve and settle, refusing past the limit, a JSON round trip, and `countingFetch` throwing at the limit.\n5. **TDD:** update the §7.3 subrequest column and the `quickCheck` line, and add a decisions-log row.\n6. **Close:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test`, commit `WP-04: …`, push, then the board ✅ with the hash and hours, commit and push.\n\n## Files\nNew: `worker/lib/doh.ts`, `worker/safety/{iprange,ssrf,reader,origin}.ts`, `worker/loop/budget.ts`, `test/unit/{ssrf,reader,origin,budget}.test.ts`. Edited: `worker/env.ts`, `.dev.vars.example`, `TDD.md` (§7.3), `TASKS.md`, `PROMPTS.md`.\n\n## Verification\n- `pnpm test`: the new unit tables pass, the SSRF table has at least 40 cases, and the existing 35 tests still pass.\n- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` are clean (the floating-promise rules matter for the stream-reading code).\n- No route uses the guard yet. It's wired into the tools in WP-05, and the live refusal check (`169.254.169.254`, `localhost`, `10.0.0.1`) happens in WP-13.\n"
}

## Approved Plan:
# WP-04 Safety core: SSRF guard, reader, origin pin, budget

## Context
WP-05's tools (`dns_lookup`, `http_probe`, `quickCheck`) fetch URLs that users and the LLM choose. Before any of that exists, the app needs the safety layer from TDD §11 and D6: an SSRF guard that refuses private and reserved addresses on every hop, a capped reader that never follows redirects on its own, the origin pin, and a subrequest budget (Free plan: 50 per invocation). WP-04 builds these as **pure, dependency-injected modules** (`fetch` is passed in), so they're unit-testable in node. Done when: the SSRF and origin unit tables pass.

## Design

### `worker/safety/iprange.ts`
- `parseIpv4(s) → number | null` and `parseIpv6(s) → bigint | null`. The IPv6 parser handles `::` compression and an embedded dotted IPv4 tail; brackets are stripped by the caller.
- `isBlockedIp(ip) → boolean`. It checks every TDD §11 range:
  - **IPv4:** 0/8, 10/8, 100.64/10, 127/8, 169.254/16, 172.16/12, 192.0.0/24, 192.0.2/24, 192.168/16, 198.18/15, 198.51.100/24, 203.0.113/24, 224/4, 240/4.
  - **IPv6:** ::/128, ::1, fc00::/7, fe80::/10, ff00::/8, 64:ff9b::/96, 2001:db8::/32.
  - **IPv4-mapped (`::ffff:0:0/96`):** the embedded v4 address is checked against the v4 list.
- **Fails closed:** anything that doesn't parse counts as blocked.

### `worker/safety/ssrf.ts`
1. **`checkUrlShape(url, env)`** (no network) → `{ok:true, url: URL} | {ok:false, reason}`:
   - **Dev exception first:** if `env.SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` is set and `new URL(url).origin === it` (exact scheme, host and port), allow it.
   - Only `http:` and `https:`.
   - Port empty, 80 or 443.
   - No IP literals: v4, or bracketed v6. WHATWG `URL` already turns `http://2130706433/` and `0x7f.1` into dotted v4, so those are caught too.
   - No `localhost`, `*.localhost`, `*.local`, `*.internal`, and no single-label hosts (intranet names).
   - Any credentials in the URL are rejected.
   - Reasons are short codes plus readable text, for example `private_address`: "169.254.169.254 is a private or reserved address".
2. **`resolveAndCheck(host, deps)`**: DoH JSON lookups for A **and** AAAA (`cloudflare-dns.com`), reusing `lib/doh.ts`:
   - Blocked if **any** A/AAAA answer is in a blocked range (CNAME answers are skipped).
   - `{ok:false, reason:"no_addresses"}` if there are none. The caller treats that as a DNS failure, not an SSRF refusal.
3. **`createGuard(deps)`** → `guard(url)`, which runs 1 then 2 with a **per-tool-call cache** of host results. So each distinct host costs at most 2 DoH subrequests, however many samples or hops hit it.

### `worker/lib/doh.ts`
- `dohQuery(fetch, resolverUrl, name, type) → {status, answers:[{name,type,ttl,data}]}`.
- Shared with WP-05 `dns_lookup`, which calls both resolvers.

### `worker/safety/reader.ts`
- **Constants:** `READ_CAP = 16 KB`, `FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 10 000`, `MAX_HOPS = 5`.
- **`readOnce(fetch, url, method)`:**
  - `redirect: "manual"`, `AbortSignal.timeout`, a fixed `user-agent`, and no user headers or body.
  - Streams at most 16 KB, then cancels.
  - Returns `{status, headers, location, body, truncated, ttfbMs, totalMs}` or `{error: "timeout"|"network", message}`.
- **`followHops(fetch, guard, url, method)`:**
  - Calls `guard` on **every** hop URL, then `readOnce`.
  - Follows `Location` (relative locations are resolved) up to 5 hops.
  - Stops on a loop (a URL seen before), the hop cap, or a guard refusal.
  - Returns `{hops:[{url,status,location,ttfbMs,totalMs}], final, stop: "done"|"loop"|"max_hops"|"blocked"|"error", blockedReason?}`.
- WP-05 builds samples, header picks and snippets on top of this.

### `worker/safety/origin.ts`
- `assertInScope(tool, args, scope)` with `scope = { host, cnameTargets: string[] }`. It throws `OutOfScopeError(reason)`, which WP-09 counts as an invalid call (FR-12).
- **`http_probe`:** the URL's host must equal the incident host, case-insensitive. Any path is allowed.
- **`dns_lookup` and `cert_info`:** the hostname must be the incident host or one of this incident's CNAME targets.
- **`provider_status`:** always in scope (zod enum).
- **Unknown tool:** throws.

### `worker/loop/budget.ts`
- The TDD §5.2 loop constants (`MAX_TOOL_CALLS`, `MAX_PLAN_TURNS`, `RUN_DEADLINE_MS`, `SUBREQUEST_BUDGET = 45`, `HTTP_SAMPLES`, …).
- **`SubrequestBudget`:** a serialisable `{limit, used}` (it lives in the run row between Workflow steps), with `remaining()`, `reserve(n) → boolean` (the worst case is reserved before a tool runs), `settle(reserved, actual)`, and `toJSON`/`from`.
- **`countingFetch(fetch, budget)`:** wraps `fetch`, adds 1 per call and throws `BudgetExceededError` when the limit is reached. Used for actual counting, including by cron in WP-18.

### TDD fold-in (logged in TASKS)
§7.3's maximum subrequests didn't include the guard's DNS lookups. Updated estimates:
- **`http_probe`:** `3 × (1 + 5) + 1 + 2 × distinct hosts`.
- **`quickCheck`:** about 8 (1 A query plus the guard's AAAA, plus 6 fetches), more if redirects go to other hosts.
- The per-run reservation and `countingFetch` keep runs within 45 and cron within 50.

## Steps
1. **Board:** WP-04 🔄 in TASKS and "Now", and log the prompt in PROMPTS.
2. Write `lib/doh.ts`, `safety/iprange.ts`, `safety/ssrf.ts`, `safety/reader.ts`, `safety/origin.ts` and `loop/budget.ts` as designed.
3. Add `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` as an **optional** string in `worker/env.ts`, extending the generated `Env`. It's not added to `wrangler.jsonc` `vars`, and `.dev.vars.example` gets a commented line pointing to WP-11.
4. **Tests** (`test/unit/`, node, with a fake `fetch`):
   - **`ssrf.test.ts` (40+ cases):**
     - an IP table: every blocked range at its edges, plus allowed public v4 and v6 (1.1.1.1, 8.8.8.8, 2606:4700::1111);
     - mapped v4 (`::ffff:10.0.0.1` blocked, `::ffff:1.1.1.1` allowed);
     - URL shape: schemes, ports, literals, `[::1]`, decimal and hex IPs, localhost variants, `.internal`, single-label hosts, credentials;
     - DoH: public, any-private-in-the-mix, AAAA-only private, CNAME then public, no answers;
     - the cache (2 DoH calls for repeated hosts);
     - a hop re-check (a public URL redirecting to `http://169.254.169.254/` → `blocked` at hop 2);
     - **dev allow-origin:** on (exact match allowed), off (blocked), near-misses (other port, other scheme, subdomain) blocked.
   - **`reader.test.ts`:** 16 KB cap (`truncated`), timeout → `error: "timeout"`, the redirect loop, the 5-hop cap, relative `Location`, and that no redirect is ever followed automatically.
   - **`origin.test.ts`:** the host matches with any path, another host is refused, CNAME targets are allowed for `dns_lookup`/`cert_info` but not for `http_probe`, case-insensitivity, and an unknown tool.
   - **`budget.test.ts`:** reserve and settle, refusing past the limit, a JSON round trip, and `countingFetch` throwing at the limit.
5. **TDD:** update the §7.3 subrequest column and the `quickCheck` line, and add a decisions-log row.
6. **Close:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test`, commit `WP-04: …`, push, then the board ✅ with the hash and hours, commit and push.

## Files
New: `worker/lib/doh.ts`, `worker/safety/{iprange,ssrf,reader,origin}.ts`, `worker/loop/budget.ts`, `test/unit/{ssrf,reader,origin,budget}.test.ts`. Edited: `worker/env.ts`, `.dev.vars.example`, `TDD.md` (§7.3), `TASKS.md`, `PROMPTS.md`.

## Verification
- `pnpm test`: the new unit tables pass, the SSRF table has at least 40 cases, and the existing 35 tests still pass.
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` are clean (the floating-promise rules matter for the stream-reading code).
- No route uses the guard yet. It's wired into the tools in WP-05, and the live refusal check (`169.254.169.254`, `localhost`, `10.0.0.1`) happens in WP-13.

halves[0].split(\":\") : [];\n  let groups: string[];\n  if (halves.length === 2) {\n    const tail = halves[1] ? halves[1].split(\":\") : [];\n    if (head.length + tail.length > 7) return null;\n    groups = [...head, ...Array<string>(8 - head.length - tail.length).fill(\"0\"), ...tail];\n  } else {\n    groups = head;\n  }\n  if (groups.length !== 8) return null;\n\n  let n = 0n;\n  for (const g of groups) {\n    if (!/^[0-9a-f]{1,4}$/.test(g)) return null;\n    n = (n << 16n) | BigInt(parseInt(g, 16));\n  }\n  return n;\n}\n\ntype V4Range = { base: number; mask: number };\ntype V6Range = { base: bigint; mask: bigint };\n\nfunction v4(cidr: string): V4Range {\n  const [addr, len] = cidr.split(\"/\") as [string, string];\n  const bits = Number(len);\n  const mask = bits === 0 ? 0 : (0xffffffff << (32 - bits)) >>> 0;\n  return { base: (parseIpv4(addr)! & mask) >>> 0, mask };\n}\n\nfunction v6(cidr: string): V6Range {\n  const [addr, len] = cidr.split(\"/\") as [string, string];\n  const bits = BigInt(len);\n  const mask = ((1n << bits) - 1n) << (128n - bits);\n  return { base: parseIpv6(addr)! & mask, mask };\n}\n\nconst BLOCKED_V4 = [\n  \"0.0.0.0/8\", \"10.0.0.0/8\", \"100.64.0.0/10\", \"127.0.0.0/8\", \"169.254.0.0/16\", \"172.16.0.0/12\",\n  \"192.0.0.0/24\", \"192.0.2.0/24\", \"192.168.0.0/16\", \"198.18.0.0/15\", \"198.51.100.0/24\",\n  \"203.0.113.0/24\", \"224.0.0.0/4\", \"240.0.0.0/4\",\n].map(v4);\n\n// TDD §11 list, plus ::/96 (IPv4-compatible, covers ::/128 and ::1) and 100::/64 (discard-only).\nconst BLOCKED_V6 = [\"::/96\", \"100::/64\", \"fc00::/7\", \"fe80::/10\", \"ff00::/8\", \"64:ff9b::/96\", \"2001:db8::/32\"].map(v6);\n\nconst MAPPED_V4 = v6(\"::ffff:0:0/96\");\nconst SIX_TO_FOUR = v6(\"2002::/16\");\n\nexport function isBlockedV4(n: number): boolean {\n  return BLOCKED_V4.some((r) => ((n & r.mask) >>> 0) === r.base);\n}\n\nexport function isBlockedV6(n: bigint): boolean {\n  if ((n & MAPPED_V4.mask) === MAPPED_V4.base) return isBlockedV4(Number(n & 0xffffffffn));\n  if ((n & SIX_TO_FOUR.mask) === SIX_TO_FOUR.base) return isBlockedV4(Number((n >> 80n) & 0xffffffffn));\n  return BLOCKED_V6.some((r) => (n & r.mask) === r.base);\n}\n\n/** True for private/reserved addresses and for anything that isn't a valid IP (fail closed). */\nexport function isBlockedIp(ip: string): boolean {\n  const a = parseIpv4(ip);\n  if (a !== null) return isBlockedV4(a);\n  const b = parseIpv6(ip);\n  if (b !== null) return isBlockedV6(b);\n  return true;\n}\nEOF\necho ok",

value : undefined;\n}\n\nfunction sameOrigin(url: URL, allowed: string | undefined): boolean {\n  if (!allowed) return false;\n  try {\n    return url.origin === new URL(allowed).origin;\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\nexport function checkUrlShape(raw: string, allowOrigin?: string): GuardResult {\n  let url: URL;\n  try {\n    url = new URL(raw);\n  } catch {\n    return fail(\"invalid_url\", \"That isn't a valid URL.\");\n  }\n  if (sameOrigin(url, allowOrigin)) return { ok: true, url, devAllowed: true };\n\n  if (url.protocol !== \"http:\" && url.protocol !== \"https:\") {\n    return fail(\"scheme\", `Only http and https are allowed, not ${url.protocol.replace(\":\", \"\")}.`);\n  }\n  if (!ALLOWED_PORTS.has(url.port)) return fail(\"port\", `Only ports 80 and 443 are allowed, not ${url.port}.`);\n  if (url.username || url.password) return fail(\"credentials\", \"URLs with credentials aren't allowed.\");\n\n  const host = url.hostname.replace(/\\.$/, \"\");\n  if (host.startsWith(\"[\") || parseIpv4(host) !== null) {\n    const kind = isBlockedIp(host) ? (() => Date.now());\n  const start = now();\n  try {\n    const res = await fetchFn(url, {\n      method,\n      redirect: \"manual\",\n      headers: { \"user-agent\": USER_AGENT, accept: \"*/*\" },\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(opts.timeoutMs ?? READ_CAP);\n    return {\n      ok: true,\n      url,\n      status: res.status,\n      headers: res.headers,\n      location: res.headers.get(\"location\"),\n      body,\n      truncated,\n      ttfbMs,\n      totalMs: now() - start,\n    };\n  } catch (e) {\n    const err = e as Error;\n    const timeout = err.name === \"TimeoutError\" || err.name === \"AbortError\";\n    return { ok: false, url, error: timeout ? \"timeout\" : \"network\", message: err.message, totalMs: now() - start };\n  }\n}\n\nasync function readCapped(res: Response, cap: number): Promise<{ body: string; truncated: boolean }> {\n  if (!res.body) return { body: \"\", truncated: false };\n  const reader = res.body.getReader();\n  const chunks: Uint8Array[] = [];\n  let size = 0;\n  let truncated = false;\n  for (;;) {\n    const { done, value } = await reader.read();\n    if (done) break;\n    const room = cap - size;\n    if (value.byteLength > room) {\n      chunks.push(value.subarray(0, room));\n      size += room;\n      truncated = true;\n      break;\n    }\n    chunks.push(value);\n    size += value.byteLength;\n  }\n  if (truncated) await reader.cancel().catch(() => undefined);\n  const all = new Uint8Array(size);\n  let offset = 0;\n  for (const c of chunks) {\n    all.set(c, offset);\n    offset += c.byteLength;\n  }\n  return { body: new TextDecoder().decode(all), truncated };\n}\n\nexport type Hop = { url: string; status: number; location: string | null; ttfbMs: number; totalMs: number };\nexport type HopStop = \"done\" | \"loop\" | \"max_hops\" | \"blocked\" | \"error\";\nexport type HopResult = {\n  hops: Hop[];\n  /** Last response read (the final page, or the last redirect when stopped early). */\n  final: ReadOk | null;\n  stop: HopStop;\n  blocked?: GuardFail & { url: string };\n  error?: ReadErr | { ok: false; url: string; error: \"bad_location\"; message: string };\n};\n\nconst REDIRECTS = new Set([301, 302, 303, 307, 308]);\n\n/** Follows redirects by hand: at most 1 + MAX_HOPS requests, guard-checked each time, stopping on a loop. */\nexport async function followHops(fetchFn: Fetch, guard: Guard, startUrl: string, method: Method = \"GET\", opts: ReadOptions = {}): Promise<HopResult> {\n  const hops: Hop[] = [];\n  const seen = new Set<string>();\n  let current = startUrl;\n  let final: ReadOk | null = null;\n\n  for (let i = 0; i <= MAX_HOPS; i++) {\n    if (seen.has(current)) return { hops, final, stop: \"loop\" };\n    seen.add(current);\n\n    const checked = await guard(current);\n    if (!checked.ok) return { hops, final, stop: \"blocked\", blocked: { ...checked, url: current } };\n\n    const res = await readOnce(fetchFn, checked.url.toString(), method, opts);\n    if (!res.ok) return { hops, final, stop: \"error\", error: res };\n    final = res;\n    hops.push({ url: res.url, status: res.status, location: res.location, ttfbMs: res.ttfbMs, totalMs: res.totalMs });\n\n    if (!REDIRECTS.has(res.status) || !res.location) return { hops, final, stop: \"done\" };\n    try {\n      current = new URL(res.location, res.url).toString();\n    } catch {\n      return { hops, final, stop: \"error\", error: { ok: false, url: res.url, error: \"bad_location\", message: res.location } };\n    }\n  }\n  return { hops, final, stop: \"max_hops\" };\n}\nEOF\necho ok",

{

A violation counts as an invalid call (FR-12).\n */\nexport type Scope = { host: string; cnameTargets: readonly string[] };\n\nexport class OutOfScopeError extends Error {\n  override name = \"OutOfScopeError\";\n}\n\nconst normHost = (h: string) => h.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\\.$/, \"\");\n\nfunction hostOf(url: unknown): string {\n  if (typeof url !== \"string\") throw new OutOfScopeError(\"http_probe needs a url string.\");\n  try {\n    return normHost(new URL(url).hostname);\n  } catch {\n    throw new OutOfScopeError(`${url} isn't a valid URL.`);\n  }\n}\n\nexport function assertInScope(tool: string, args: unknown, scope: Scope): void {\n  const incidentHost = normHost(scope.host);\n  const a = (args ?? {}) as { url?: unknown; hostname?: unknown };\n\n  switch (tool) {\n    case \"http_probe\": {\n      const host = hostOf(a.url);\n      if (host !== incidentHost) {\n        throw new OutOfScopeError(`http_probe is limited to ${incidentHost}; ${host} needs its own incident.`);\n      }\n      return;\n    }\n    case \"dns_lookup\":\n    case \"cert_info\": {\n      if (typeof a.hostname !== \"string\") throw new OutOfScopeError(`${tool} needs a hostname string.`);\n      const host = normHost(a.hostname);\n      const allowed = new Set([incidentHost, ...scope.cnameTargets.map(normHost)]);\n      if (!allowed.has(host)) {\n        throw new OutOfScopeError(`${tool} is limited to ${incidentHost} and its CNAME targets; ${host} isn't one.`);\n      }\n      return;\n    }\n    case \"provider_status\":\n    case \"submit_findings\":\n      return;\n    default:\n      throw new OutOfScopeError(`Unknown tool ${tool}.`);\n  }\n}\nEOF\ncat > worker/loop/budget.ts <<'EOF'\nimport type { Fetch } from \"../lib/doh\";\n\n/** Run bounds (TDD §5.2). */\nexport const MAX_TOOL_CALLS = 6;\nexport const MAX_PLAN_TURNS = 3;\nexport const RUN_DEADLINE_MS = 60_000;\nexport const SUBREQUEST_BUDGET = 45;\nexport const HTTP_SAMPLES = 3;\nexport const SNIPPET_TO_LLM = 500;\nexport const EVIDENCE_DIGEST_MAX = 1536;\nexport const HIGH_LATENCY_TOTAL_MS = 2000;\nexport const HIGH_LATENCY_TTFB_MS = 1500;\n\nexport class BudgetExceededError extends Error {\n  override name = \"BudgetExceededError\";\n}\n\n/**\n * Per-run subrequest budget. Serialisable so it can live in the run row between Workflow steps.\n * A tool reserves its worst case up front, then settles with what it actually used.\n */\nexport class SubrequestBudget {\n  constructor(\n    readonly limit: number = SUBREQUEST_BUDGET,\n    private used = 0,\n    private reserved = 0,\n  ) {}\n\n  static from(json: { limit: number; used: number; reserved?: number }): SubrequestBudget {\n    return new SubrequestBudget(json.limit, json.used, json.reserved ?? 0);\n  }\n\n  toJSON() {\n    return { limit: this.limit, used: this.used, reserved: this.reserved };\n  }\n\n  get spent(): number {\n    return this.used;\n  }\n\n  remaining(): number {\n    return this.limit - this.used - this.reserved;\n  }\n\n  reserve(n: number): boolean {\n    if (n > this.remaining()) return false;\n    this.reserved += n;\n    return true;\n  }\n\n  settle(reserved: number, actual: number): void {\n    this.reserved = Math.max(0, this.reserved - reserved);\n    this.used += actual;\n  }\n}\n\n/** Counts real outbound requests against a hard cap; used inside a tool call and by cron. */\nexport class SubrequestCounter {\n  count = 0;\n  constructor(readonly limit: number) {}\n}\n\nexport function countingFetch(fetchFn: Fetch, counter: SubrequestCounter): Fetch {\n  return (input, init) => {\n    if (counter.count >= counter.limit) {\n      return Promise.reject(new BudgetExceededError(`Subrequest limit of ${counter.limit} reached.`));\n    }\n    counter.count++;\n    return fetchFn(input, init);\n  };\n}\nEOF\ncat >> .dev.vars.example <<'EOF'\n# WP-11: one exact origin (scheme://host:port) the SSRF guard lets through locally, e.g. the local demo.\n# Never set this in wrangler.jsonc vars (TDD §11).\n# SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN=http://localhost:8788\nEOF\npnpm typecheck 2>&1 | tail -10",

}

### 🤖 Claude · 01:30:44

WP-04 is done and pushed (`4dbe27d`, board `7ab930e`). It took about 0.25 h against the 1.0 h estimate. All the pieces below fail closed: anything they can't parse or resolve is refused.

**What's in place:**
- **Private-address guard (`ssrf.ts`):** before any fetch it refuses:
  - other schemes;
  - ports other than 80 and 443;
  - raw IP addresses, including tricks like `http://2130706433/`;
  - `localhost`, `.local` and `.internal` names;
  - single-word intranet names;
  - URLs with a username or password.

  It then looks up the host's IPv4 and IPv6 addresses and refuses the host if any of them is private or reserved. Each host is looked up once per tool call, so repeated samples don't spend extra calls. Refusals come with plain messages, for example "169.254.169.254 is a private or reserved address".
- **Your local-demo exception:** `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` lets exactly one origin through. Tests confirm that a different port, scheme or subdomain is still blocked, and that it does nothing when unset.
- **Reader (`reader.ts`):**
  - reads at most 16 KB;
  - times out after 10 s;
  - never follows redirects on its own;
  - follows them by hand and re-checks every redirect target;
  - stops on a loop or after 5 redirects.

  A test confirms that a public site redirecting to `169.254.169.254` is stopped at that redirect.
- **Origin pin (`origin.ts`):** probes stay on the incident's own host, and DNS and certificate lookups may also use its CNAME targets. Anything else is refused with a message naming the other host, so the UI can offer a new incident for it.
- **Budget (`budget.ts`):** a call budget that can be saved and resumed between Workflow steps, where each tool reserves its worst case up front. Also a call counter that refuses once the limit is reached, for cron to use later.

**Checks:** 151 new unit tests (118 of them for the guard), 186 in total. Typecheck and lint are clean.

**What changed in the TDD:**
- **Cost estimates:** the TDD's subrequest estimates didn't count the guard's own DNS lookups, so I raised them. `quickCheck` now costs about 8 calls instead of 7, which still fits cron's batch of 6 under the limit of 50.
- **Extra blocked ranges:** I added three IPv6 forms that can reach private IPv4 addresses.

Both changes are in TDD §7.3 and §11 and in the decisions log.

Next is WP-05, the tools: `dns_lookup`, `http_probe`, `quickCheck` and the tool registry.

WP-05 builds those checks on top of the WP-04 safety layer (`createGuard`, `followHops`, `readOnce`, `dohQuery`, `assertInScope`, `countingFetch`): two tools with zod inputs, compact LLM digests (≤ 1.5 KB) and full raw evidence for the UI, plus `quickCheck` and a registry that the LLM layer (WP-07) turns into tool definitions. Done when: the parser tests pass and probes of a real host work from `wrangler dev`.\n\n## Decisions\n- **`dns_lookup` mismatch = real disagreement only (user decision).** `mismatch` is true when the resolvers disagree on the response code, or on whether the name has any A/AAAA address. Different address lists (normal with geo/CDN DNS) are reported as `addressesDiffer`, for information only. TDD §9.3 rule 9 and §7.3 are updated to match.\n- **Per-tool subrequest caps enforced by `countingFetch`:**\n  - `dns_lookup` = 2 × types;\n  - `http_probe` = **20** (2 guard DoH + 3 × 6 fetches; the asset sub-probe is skipped if there's no room left);\n  - `quickCheck` = 10 by default.\n\n  If a cap is hit, the result is partial and marked `budget`, never an exception.\n- **No extra sampling when it can't help:** if sample 1 ends in a `loop`, `max_hops` or `blocked`, samples 2–3 are skipped, because those outcomes are deterministic.\n- **Live check from `wrangler dev`:** a temporary `/api/dev/probe` route, used once for verification and **removed before the commit**, the same way as the WP-02 spike.\n\n## Design\n- **`worker/tools/types.ts`:**\n  - `ToolName`, `CheckStatus = \"pass\"|\"fail\"|\"error\"|\"unavailable\"` (matches TDD §6.3 `CheckView`), and `ToolResult { evidenceId, tool, ok, status, summary, digest: string, raw }`.\n  - `ToolContext { fetch, evidenceId, allowOrigin?, rateLimit?(host) → Promise<boolean>, now?() }`. `rateLimit` is a hook for WP-17's `PROBE_LIMIT`; its default allows everything.\n- **`worker/tools/digest.ts`:** `fitDigest(obj, max = 1536)` serialises compact JSON and drops optional fields in a fixed order until it fits (the snippet is shortened first). `untrusted(text, n = 500)` collapses whitespace, strips tags, cuts the text to `n` and escapes any `</untrusted>` inside it, then wraps the result in `<untrusted>…</untrusted>`.\n- **`worker/tools/dns.ts`:**\n  - **Input:** `{hostname (zod hostname regex, no IP literals), types = [\"A\",\"AAAA\"]}`.\n  - **Queries:** each type on **both** resolvers (`RESOLVERS.cloudflare`, `RESOLVERS.google`) in parallel through `dohQuery`.\n  - **Raw:** per resolver and type, the status name (NOERROR/NXDOMAIN/SERVFAIL/…), answers (data and TTL), the CNAME chain, plus `cnameTargets` (WP-08 feeds these to the origin pin), `mismatch`, `addressesDiffer`, and `ssrfBlocked` (any answer `isBlockedIp`).\n  - **Status:**\n    - `pass` when both resolvers return NOERROR with at least one address;\n    - `fail` on NXDOMAIN, SERVFAIL, no addresses, or a mismatch;\n    - `unavailable` when both resolvers error.\n  - **Summary:** for example \"A 93.184.215.14 · both resolvers agree\".\n- **`worker/tools/http.ts`:**\n  - **Input:** `{url (http/https), method = \"GET\"}`.\n  - **Samples:** one guard per call (`createGuard`, cached DNS). Up to 3 samples run **one after another**, so the latency figures are real. `rateLimit(host)` runs before each sample, and `followHops` runs per sample.\n  - **Per sample:** final status, TTFB of the final hop, total ms (all hops) and the stop reason. The median total and median TTFB are computed across samples.\n  - **Hop list:** from sample 1.\n  - **Picked headers** (from the final response of the first sample that got one): `server`, `cf-ray`, `cf-cache-status`, `cache-control`, `content-type`, `location`, and whether `set-cookie` is present (cookie values are never stored).\n  - **Snippet:** `untrusted()` over the final body, for text content types only.\n  - **Asset sub-probe (D10):** if the final response is HTML, take the first same-origin `<script src>` or `<link rel=stylesheet href>` and resolve it against the final URL. The guard checks it, then `readOnce` fetches it once. The result records `{url, status, cacheControl, setCookie}`.\n  - **Status:**\n    - `error` if the guard refused the URL (summary \"Refused: …\");\n    - `unavailable` if rate-limited or the budget ran out before any sample;\n    - `fail` on 5xx, a timeout or network error, `loop` or `max_hops`, or a median above `HIGH_LATENCY_*`;\n    - otherwise `pass`.\n  - **Raw:** samples, hops, headers, a 2 KB snippet and the asset result. The digest is the compact subset, fitted to 1.5 KB.\n- **`worker/tools/quickcheck.ts`:** `quickCheck(url, {fetch, allowOrigin, now})` makes one `followHops` GET through a guard. It returns `{pass, status, totalMs, stop, reason}`, where `pass` means a final 2xx/3xx, `stop === \"done\"`, and a total under `HIGH_LATENCY_TOTAL_MS`.\n- **`worker/tools/registry.ts`:**\n  - `TOOLS` (`dns_lookup`, `http_probe`, each with `label`, `prefix`, zod `input`, `maxSubrequests(input)` and `run`).\n  - `evidenceId(prefix, n)` with prefixes `dns`/`http`/`cert`/`status` (TDD §6.4); WP-08 allocates the numbers.\n  - `llmTools()` produces OpenAI function definitions via `z.toJSONSchema` (WP-07 adds `submit_findings`).\n  - `runTool(name, rawArgs, ctx, scope)`: zod parse, then `assertInScope`, then `run` with `countingFetch(ctx.fetch, new SubrequestCounter(cap))`. It returns `{result, subrequests}`. An invalid or out-of-scope call returns `{invalid: reason}` (FR-12).\n  - `cert_info` and `provider_status` slot in at WP-21.\n\n## Steps\n1. **Board:** WP-05 🔄 and update \"Now\"; log the prompt and the mismatch answer in PROMPTS.\n2. NXDOMAIN on both → `fail`; SERVFAIL;\n     - one resolver NXDOMAIN and the other answering → `mismatch`;\n     - different address lists → `addressesDiffer` but **no** mismatch and still `pass`;\n     - CNAME chain → `cnameTargets`; 5xx → `fail`;\n     - a redirect loop stops after sample 1 with `loop`; a private redirect → `error`;\n     - header picks and set-cookie presence; snippet wrapping and escaping;\n     - asset extraction (first same-origin script or stylesheet, cross-origin skipped, `bad_cache` headers recorded);\n     - `rateLimit` → `unavailable`; ## Approved Plan:
# WP-05 Tools: `dns_lookup`, `http_probe`, `quickCheck`, registry

## Context
The diagnosis loop (WP-09), the rules (WP-06), cron (WP-18) and verify (WP-12) all run checks. WP-05 builds those checks on top of the WP-04 safety layer (`createGuard`, `followHops`, `readOnce`, `dohQuery`, `assertInScope`, `countingFetch`): two tools with zod inputs, compact LLM digests (≤ 1.5 KB) and full raw evidence for the UI, plus `quickCheck` and a registry that the LLM layer (WP-07) turns into tool definitions. Done when: the parser tests pass and probes of a real host work from `wrangler dev`.

## Decisions
- **`dns_lookup` mismatch = real disagreement only (user decision).** `mismatch` is true when the resolvers disagree on the response code, or on whether the name has any A/AAAA address. Different address lists (normal with geo/CDN DNS) are reported as `addressesDiffer`, for information only. TDD §9.3 rule 9 and §7.3 are updated to match.
- **Per-tool subrequest caps enforced by `countingFetch`:**
  - `dns_lookup` = 2 × types;
  - `http_probe` = **20** (2 guard DoH + 3 × 6 fetches; the asset sub-probe is skipped if there's no room left);
  - `quickCheck` = 10 by default.

  If a cap is hit, the result is partial and marked `budget`, never an exception.
- **No extra sampling when it can't help:** if sample 1 ends in a `loop`, `max_hops` or `blocked`, samples 2–3 are skipped, because those outcomes are deterministic.
- **Live check from `wrangler dev`:** a temporary `/api/dev/probe` route, used once for verification and **removed before the commit**, the same way as the WP-02 spike.

## Design
- **`worker/tools/types.ts`:**
  - `ToolName`, `CheckStatus = "pass"|"fail"|"error"|"unavailable"` (matches TDD §6.3 `CheckView`), and `ToolResult { evidenceId, tool, ok, status, summary, digest: string, raw }`.
  - `ToolContext { fetch, evidenceId, allowOrigin?, rateLimit?(host) → Promise<boolean>, now?() }`. `rateLimit` is a hook for WP-17's `PROBE_LIMIT`; its default allows everything.
- **`worker/tools/digest.ts`:** `fitDigest(obj, max = 1536)` serialises compact JSON and drops optional fields in a fixed order until it fits (the snippet is shortened first). `untrusted(text, n = 500)` collapses whitespace, strips tags, cuts the text to `n` and escapes any `</untrusted>` inside it, then wraps the result in `<untrusted>…</untrusted>`.
- **`worker/tools/dns.ts`:**
  - **Input:** `{hostname (zod hostname regex, no IP literals), types = ["A","AAAA"]}`.
  - **Queries:** each type on **both** resolvers (`RESOLVERS.cloudflare`, `RESOLVERS.google`) in parallel through `dohQuery`.
  - **Raw:** per resolver and type, the status name (NOERROR/NXDOMAIN/SERVFAIL/…), answers (data and TTL), the CNAME chain, plus `cnameTargets` (WP-08 feeds these to the origin pin), `mismatch`, `addressesDiffer`, and `ssrfBlocked` (any answer `isBlockedIp`).
  - **Status:**
    - `pass` when both resolvers return NOERROR with at least one address;
    - `fail` on NXDOMAIN, SERVFAIL, no addresses, or a mismatch;
    - `unavailable` when both resolvers error.
  - **Summary:** for example "A 93.184.215.14 · both resolvers agree".
- **`worker/tools/http.ts`:**
  - **Input:** `{url (http/https), method = "GET"}`.
  - **Samples:** one guard per call (`createGuard`, cached DNS). Up to 3 samples run **one after another**, so the latency figures are real. `rateLimit(host)` runs before each sample, and `followHops` runs per sample.
  - **Per sample:** final status, TTFB of the final hop, total ms (all hops) and the stop reason. The median total and median TTFB are computed across samples.
  - **Hop list:** from sample 1.
  - **Picked headers** (from the final response of the first sample that got one): `server`, `cf-ray`, `cf-cache-status`, `cache-control`, `content-type`, `location`, and whether `set-cookie` is present (cookie values are never stored).
  - **Snippet:** `untrusted()` over the final body, for text content types only.
  - **Asset sub-probe (D10):** if the final response is HTML, take the first same-origin `<script src>` or `<link rel=stylesheet href>` and resolve it against the final URL. The guard checks it, then `readOnce` fetches it once. The result records `{url, status, cacheControl, setCookie}`.
  - **Status:**
    - `error` if the guard refused the URL (summary "Refused: …");
    - `unavailable` if rate-limited or the budget ran out before any sample;
    - `fail` on 5xx, a timeout or network error, `loop` or `max_hops`, or a median above `HIGH_LATENCY_*`;
    - otherwise `pass`.
  - **Raw:** samples, hops, headers, a 2 KB snippet and the asset result. The digest is the compact subset, fitted to 1.5 KB.
- **`worker/tools/quickcheck.ts`:** `quickCheck(url, {fetch, allowOrigin, now})` makes one `followHops` GET through a guard. It returns `{pass, status, totalMs, stop, reason}`, where `pass` means a final 2xx/3xx, `stop === "done"`, and a total under `HIGH_LATENCY_TOTAL_MS`.
- **`worker/tools/registry.ts`:**
  - `TOOLS` (`dns_lookup`, `http_probe`, each with `label`, `prefix`, zod `input`, `maxSubrequests(input)` and `run`).
  - `evidenceId(prefix, n)` with prefixes `dns`/`http`/`cert`/`status` (TDD §6.4); WP-08 allocates the numbers.
  - `llmTools()` produces OpenAI function definitions via `z.toJSONSchema` (WP-07 adds `submit_findings`).
  - `runTool(name, rawArgs, ctx, scope)`: zod parse, then `assertInScope`, then `run` with `countingFetch(ctx.fetch, new SubrequestCounter(cap))`. It returns `{result, subrequests}`. An invalid or out-of-scope call returns `{invalid: reason}` (FR-12).
  - `cert_info` and `provider_status` slot in at WP-21.

## Steps
1. **Board:** WP-05 🔄 and update "Now"; log the prompt and the mismatch answer in PROMPTS.
2. Write `tools/{types,digest,dns,http,quickcheck,registry}.ts`.
3. **Tests** (`test/unit/`, fake fetch and fake clock via `ctx.now`/`opts.now`; reuse `test/unit/helpers/fake-fetch.ts`, extended with a scripted multi-host router):
   - **`dns.test.ts`:**
     - NOERROR on both → `pass`; NXDOMAIN on both → `fail`; SERVFAIL;
     - one resolver NXDOMAIN and the other answering → `mismatch`;
     - different address lists → `addressesDiffer` but **no** mismatch and still `pass`;
     - CNAME chain → `cnameTargets`; a private answer → `ssrfBlocked`;
     - both resolvers erroring → `unavailable`; 2 × types requests; digest ≤ 1536.
   - **`http.test.ts`:**
     - 3 samples and medians; slow responses → `fail` (high latency); 5xx → `fail`;
     - a redirect loop stops after sample 1 with `loop`; a private redirect → `error`;
     - header picks and set-cookie presence; snippet wrapping and escaping;
     - asset extraction (first same-origin script or stylesheet, cross-origin skipped, `bad_cache` headers recorded);
     - `rateLimit` → `unavailable`; the cap of 20 respected (partial `budget`); digest ≤ 1536.
   - **`quickcheck.test.ts`:** pass, 5xx, slow, blocked.
   - **`registry.test.ts`:** zod rejects bad args, an out-of-scope call → invalid, `llmTools()` shape, evidence IDs, and the counter cap enforced.
4. **Live check:**
   - Add a temporary `GET /api/dev/probe?tool=…&url|hostname=…` to the router, then run `pnpm dev`.
   - Probe `cloudflare.com` (http and dns), `example.com`, a redirecting host (`http://github.com` → https), `http://169.254.169.254` (refused), and a host with geo DNS (`google.com`, expecting `addressesDiffer` but no mismatch).
   - Record anything surprising about `redirect: "manual"` or `global_fetch_strictly_public`, then **remove the route**.
5. **TDD:** §7.3 caps (http_probe 20, quickCheck 10, dns 2 × types), the §7.3 `dns_lookup` digest wording (mismatch vs addressesDiffer), §9.3 rule 9, and decisions-log rows.
6. **Close:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test`, then commit `WP-05: …`, push, and the board ✅ with the hash and hours, then commit and push.

## Files
New: `worker/tools/{types,digest,dns,http,quickcheck,registry}.ts`, `test/unit/{dns,http,quickcheck,registry}.test.ts`. Edited: `test/unit/helpers/fake-fetch.ts`, `TDD.md` (§7.3, §9.3), `TASKS.md`, `PROMPTS.md`. The temporary `worker/api/router.ts` dev route is added and removed within the WP.

## Verification
- `pnpm test` is green with the new tables, and every digest fixture asserts a length ≤ 1536.
- The live probes from `wrangler dev`:
  - `cloudflare.com` passes with real latencies and `cf-ray` and `server: cloudflare` picked;
  - `http://github.com` shows a 301 hop then 200;
  - `169.254.169.254` is refused;
  - `google.com` DNS passes with `addressesDiffer` but no mismatch.
- `git diff` before the commit shows no `/api/dev` route.

Returns false when limited. */\n  rateLimit?: (host: string) => Promise<boolean>;\n  now?: () => number;\n};\nEOF\ncat > worker/tools/digest.ts <<'EOF'\nexport const DIGEST_MAX = 1536;\n\nconst encoder = new TextEncoder();\nconst byteLength = (s: string) => encoder.encode(s).length;\n\n/**\n * Serialises `obj` as compact JSON, dropping keys in `dropOrder` until it fits in `max` bytes.\n * As a last resort the string is cut, so the bound always holds.\n */\nexport function fitDigest(obj: Record<string, unknown>, dropOrder: readonly string[], max = DIGEST_MAX): string {\n  const copy: Record<string, unknown> = { ...obj };\n  let out = JSON.stringify(copy);\n  for (const key of dropOrder) {\n    if (byteLength(out) <= max) return out;\n    delete copy[key];\n    out = JSON.stringify(copy);\n  }\n  while (byteLength(out) > max) out = `${out.slice(0, Math.max(0, out.length - 64))}…`;\n  return out;\n}\n\n/**\n * Text from the internet, prepared for a prompt: tags stripped, whitespace collapsed, cut to `max`\n * characters and wrapped so the model treats it as data (TDD §9.1). Nested markers are escaped.\n */\nexport function untrusted(text: string, max = 500): string {\n  const plain = text\n    .replace(/<script[\\s\\S]*?<\\/script>|<style[\\s\\S]*?<\\/style>/gi, \" \")\n    .replace(/<[^>]*>/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/\\s+/g, \" \")\n    .trim()\n    .slice(0, max)\n    .replace(/<\\/?untrusted/gi, (m) => m.replace(\"<\", \"&lt;\"));\n  return `<untrusted>${plain}</untrusted>`;\n}\nEOF\necho ok",

}

data: string; ttl: number };\nexport type TypeResult = { status: string; answers: DnsAnswer[] } | { status: \"ERROR\"; error: string; answers: [] };\n\nexport type DnsRaw = {\n  hostname: string;\n  types: string[];\n  resolvers: Record<ResolverName, Record<string, TypeResult>>;\n  addresses: Record<ResolverName, string[]>;\n  cnameChain: string[];\n  /** CNAME targets the origin pin allows for dns_lookup/cert_info (D6). */\n  cnameTargets: string[];\n  /** Resolvers disagree on the response code or on whether the name has any address. */\n  mismatch: boolean;\n  /** Both have addresses but different sets; normal for geo/CDN DNS, informational only. */\n  addressesDiffer: boolean;\n  ssrfBlocked: boolean;\n};\n\nconst stripDot = (s: string) => s.replace(/\\.$/, \"\");\n\nexport async function runDnsLookup(input: DnsInput, ctx: ToolContext): Promise<ToolResult<DnsRaw>> {\n  const jobs = RESOLVER_NAMES.flatMap((resolver) =>\n    input.types.map(async (type) => {\n      try {\n        const r = await dohQuery(ctx.fetch, RESOLVERS[resolver], input.hostname, type);\n        const result: TypeResult = {\n          status: RCODES[r.status] ?? `RCODE${r.status}`,\n          answers: r.answers.map((a) => ({ type: TYPE_NAMES[a.type] ?? e;\n        const result: TypeResult = { status: \"ERROR\", error: (e as Error).message, answers: [] };\n        return { resolver, type, result };\n      }\n    }),\n  );\n  const settled = await Promise.all(jobs);\n\n  const resolvers = { cloudflare: {}, google: {} } as DnsRaw[\"resolvers\"];\n  for (const { resolver, type, result } of settled) resolvers[resolver][type] = result;\n\n  const addresses = Object.fromEntries(\n    RESOLVER_NAMES.map((r) => [\n      r,\n      [...new Set(Object.values(resolvers[r]).flatMap((t) => t.answers.filter((a) => ADDRESS_TYPES.has(RR_TYPES[a.type as RrType])).map((a) => a.data)))],\n    ]),\n  ) as Record<ResolverName, string[]>;\n\n  const cnameChain = [\n    ...new Set(\n      RESOLVER_NAMES.flatMap((r) => Object.values(resolvers[r]).flatMap((t) => t.answers.filter((a) => a.type === \"CNAME\").map((a) => a.data))),\n    ),\n  ];\n\n  const wantsAddresses = input.types.some((t) => t === \"A\" || t === \"AAAA\");\n  const answered = (r: ResolverName) => Object.values(resolvers[r]).some((t) => t.status !== \"ERROR\");\n  const bothAnswered = RESOLVER_NAMES.every(answered);\n\n  let mismatch = false;\n  if (bothAnswered) {\n    for (const type of input.types) {\n      const a = resolvers.cloudflare[type]!;\n      const b = resolvers.google[type]!;\n      if (a.status !== \"ERROR\" && b.status !== \"ERROR\" && a.status !== b.status) mismatch = true;\n    }\n    if (wantsAddresses && addresses.cloudflare.length > 0 !== addresses.google.length > 0) mismatch = true;\n  }\n  const sorted = (xs: string[]) => [...xs].sort().join(\",\");\n  const addressesDiffer =\n    addresses.cloudflare.length > 0 && addresses.google.length > 0 && sorted(addresses.cloudflare) !== sorted(addresses.google);\n  const ssrfBlocked = RESOLVER_NAMES.some((r) => addresses[r].some((ip) => isBlockedIp(ip)));\n\n  const raw: DnsRaw = {\n    hostname: input.hostname,\n    types: input.types,\n    resolvers,\n    addresses,\n    cnameChain,\n    cnameTargets: cnameChain,\n    mismatch,\n    addressesDiffer,\n    ssrfBlocked,\n  };\n\n  const { status, summary } = judge(raw, wantsAddresses, RESOLVER_NAMES.some(answered));\n  return {\n    evidenceId: ctx.evidenceId,\n    tool: \"dns_lookup\",\n    ok: status === \"pass\" || status === \"fail\",\n    status,\n    summary,\n    digest: dnsDigest(raw),\n    raw,\n  };\n}\n\nfunction judge(raw: DnsRaw, wantsAddresses: boolean, anyAnswered: boolean): { status: CheckStatus; summary: string } {\n  if (!anyAnswered) t.status))].join(\"/\") }));\n  if (raw.mismatch) {\n    return { status: \"fail\", summary: `Resolvers disagree: ${statuses.map(({ r, s }) => `${r} ${s}`).join(\", \")}.` };\n  }\n  const bad = const addrs = raw.addresses.cloudflare.length ? raw.addresses.cloudflare : raw.addresses.google;\n  if (wantsAddresses && addrs.length === 0) return { status: \"fail\", summary: \"No A or AAAA records.\" };\n\n  const parts: string[] = [];\n  if (addrs.length) parts.push(`${addrs.slice(0, 3).join(\", \")}${addrs.length > 3 ? ` +${addrs.length - 3}` : \"\"}`);\n  if (raw.cnameChain.length) parts.push(\"resolves to a private address\");\n  return { status: \"pass\", summary: parts.join(\" · \") };\n}\n\nfunction dnsDigest(raw: DnsRaw): string {\n  const line = (t: TypeResult) => {\n    if (t.status === \"ERROR\") return \"ERROR\";\n    const data = t.answers.slice(0, 4).map((a) => `${a.type} ${a.data} ttl=${a.ttl}`);\n    if (t.answers.length > 4) data.push(`+${t.answers.length - 4} more`);\n    return [t.status, ...data].join(\" \");\n  };\n  const per = (r: ResolverName) => Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(raw.resolvers[r]).map(([type, t]) => [type, line(t)]));\n  return fitDigest(\n    {\n      host: raw.hostname,\n      cloudflare: per(\"cloudflare\"),\n      google: per(\"google\"),\n      cname: raw.cnameChain,\n      mismatch: raw.mismatch,\n      addressesDiffer: raw.addressesDiffer,\n      ssrfBlocked: raw.ssrfBlocked,\n    },\n    [\"google\", \"cname\"],\n  );\n}\nEOF\npnpm typecheck 2>&1 | tail -10",

args: unknown; cap: number; run: (ctx: ToolContext) => Promise<ToolResult> } | { ok: false; error: string };\n\nexport type RegisteredTool = {\n  name: ToolName;\n  label: string;\n  description: string;\n  parameters: () => Record<string, unknown>;\n  prepare: (args: unknown) => Prepared;\n};\n\nfunction register<S extends z.ZodType>(def: ToolDef<S>): RegisteredTool {\n  return {\n    name: def.name,\n    label: def.label,\n    description: def.description,\n    parameters: () => {\n      const { $schema: _ignored, ...schema } = z.toJSONSchema(def.input, { io: \"input\" }) as Record<string, unknown>;\n      return schema;\n    },\n    prepare: (args) => {\n      const parsed = def.input.safeParse(args);\n      if (!parsed.success) return { ok: false, error: z.prettifyError(parsed.error) };\n      const input = parsed.data;\n      return { ok: true, args: input, cap: def.maxSubrequests(input), run: (ctx) => def.run(input, ctx) };\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nexport const TOOLS: Partial<Record<ToolName, RegisteredTool>> = {\n  dns_lookup: register({\n    name: \"dns_lookup\",\n    label: \"DNS lookup\",\n    description:\n      \"Resolve DNS records for the incident host (or a CNAME target from earlier DNS evidence) via Cloudflare and Google DNS-over-HTTPS. Reports status, answers, TTLs, the CNAME chain and whether the resolvers disagree.\",\n    input: dnsInput,\n    maxSubrequests: (i) => 2 * i.types.length,\n    run: runDnsLookup,\n  }),\n  http_probe: register({\n    name: \"http_probe\",\n    label: \"HTTP probe\",\n    description:\n      \"Request a URL on the incident host 3 times. Reports status codes, time to first byte and total time, redirect hops, selected headers, an untrusted body snippet and the cache headers of one same-origin asset.\",\n    input: httpInput,\n    maxSubrequests: () => HTTP_PROBE_CAP,\n    run: runHttpProbe,\n  }),\n};\n\n/** OpenAI chat-completions tool definitions (TDD §7.4.1); WP-07 appends `submit_findings`. */\nexport function llmTools() {\n  return Object.values(TOOLS).map((t) => ({\n    type: \"function\" as const,\n    function: { name: t.name, description: t.description, parameters: t.parameters() },\n  }));\n}\n\nexport type RunOutcome = { ok: true; result: ToolResult; subrequests: number; cap: number } | { ok: false; invalid: string };\n\n/**\n * Validates args (zod), enforces the origin pin (D6) and runs the tool under its subrequest cap.\n * Invalid or out-of-scope calls come back as `invalid` so the loop can report them to the model (FR-12).\n */\nexport async function runTool(name: string, args: unknown, ctx: ToolContext, scope: Scope): Promise<RunOutcome> {\n  const tool = TOOLS[name as ToolName];\n  if (!tool) return { ok: false, invalid: `Unknown tool ${name}.` };\n  const prepared = tool.prepare(args);\n  if (!prepared.ok) return { ok: false, invalid: `Invalid arguments for ${name}: ${prepared.error}` };\n  try {\n    assertInScope(name, prepared.args, scope);\n  } catch (e) {\n    if (e instanceof OutOfScopeError) return { ok: false, invalid: e.message };\n    throw e;\n  }\n  const counter = new SubrequestCounter(prepared.cap);\n  const result = await prepared.run({ ...ctx, fetch: countingFetch(ctx.fetch, counter) });\n  return { ok: true, result, subrequests: counter.count, cap: prepared.cap };\n}\nEOF\npnpm typecheck 2>&1 | tail -15; pnpm lint 2>&1 | tail -15",
  "description": "Write quickCheck and registry, typecheck and lint",
  "timeout": 120000
}

[] : (rec.CNAME ?? [])).map((data) => ({ name, type: TYPE.CNAME, TTL: 300, data })),\n      ...((rec[type] as string[] | undefined) ?? ### 🤖 Claude · 01:44:25

WP-05 is done and pushed (`307d342`, board `28de1e4`). It took about 0.25 h against the 1.5 h estimate.

**The tools:**
- **`dns_lookup`:** asks both Cloudflare and Google DNS. Following your decision, it only flags a mismatch when the resolvers really disagree (the name exists on one but not the other, or the response codes differ). Different addresses per resolver are shown as information only. It also records the CNAME chain, which feeds the origin pin later, and flags any answer that is a private address.
- **`http_probe`:** takes 3 samples one after another for real latency figures, and reports medians, redirect hops and key headers. For cookies it records only whether one was set, never the value. Its body snippet is wrapped so the LLM treats it as untrusted data. It also checks the first same-origin script or stylesheet, which is how the demo's `bad_cache` mode gets caught. It stops after one sample if it hits a redirect loop or a blocked address, and it's capped at 20 calls; hitting the cap gives a partial result rather than an error.
- **`quickCheck`:** a cheap pass/fail for cron, monitoring and post-fix checks, capped at 10 calls. A plain 200 costs 3.
- **Registry:** builds evidence IDs like `dns-1` and `http-2`, and produces the tool descriptions the LLM needs. Every tool call goes through the same path: argument check, then the origin pin, then a capped run. Bad or off-target calls come back as "invalid" so the model can be told why.

**Bug fixed in WP-04 code:** the reader and the guard were turning "out of call budget" into a fake network or DNS failure. They now let it through so callers can tell the difference.

**Checks:** 53 new tests (239 in total), with typecheck and lint clean. The live probes from `wrangler dev` behaved as expected (summary above), and the temporary debug route is gone; I confirmed the router matches its committed state.

**What changed in the TDD:** the new DNS mismatch meaning (§7.3 and classification rule 9 in §9.3) and the per-tool call caps (§7.3). Both are in the decisions log.

Next is WP-06, the rules: `classify.ts` (the 11 categories, first match wins) and `template.ts` (the diagnosis written without the LLM).

Constants are in `worker/loop/budget.ts` (`HIGH_LATENCY_*` etc.).\n- **LLM (for WP-07):** both models use the chat-completions format. Planning turns need `tool_choice:\"required\"`; glm needs `chat_template_kwargs:{enable_thinking:false}`; usage comes from `usage.neurons`.\n- **Agents SDK 0.28.1:** use `this.workflowId` in `AgentWorkflow` (`this.instanceId` is undefined).
